# Proof packet formalization progress

The active scope is every claimed theorem in the delivered packet index.
A packet is complete when its source claims have explicit object-language
statements, checked derivations in `Prf PRAAx`, and documented assumptions
and encoding conventions. The table below indexes the whole packet scope
and the current derivation families. Each module records the mathematical
statements it proves. Source-claim reconciliation remains open across the
packet inventory.
The [complete FIBER claim audit](FIBER-CLAIMS.md) covers its theorem
environments, target signatures, implementation phases and regression
obligations, with exact statement-level status.
The [finite-demand claim audit](FINITE-DEMAND-CLAIMS.md) accounts for all 17
source theorem environments and the exact scheduling/aggregate/root/norm
producer boundaries.
The [Minkowski claim audit](MINKOWSKI-CLAIMS.md) records the complete
rational-box certificate theorem and finite-prefix pigeonhole lemma, and
distinguishes the remaining polytope/volume, equality and real-sign claims.
The [Gamma claim audit](GAMMA-CLAIMS.md) retains all 62 source-ledger families
and distinguishes finite factor/tail names from the remaining analytic endpoints.
The [number theory claim audit](NUMBER-THEORY-CLAIMS.md) matches all 106 theorem
environments of the survey packet `number_theory_ipra_2026-11-22` against the
derivations, statement by statement (73 derived, 25 in part, 8 open).

For every labelling the full Kuhn triangles plus the boundary doors are even in number; under the literal admissibility conditions `eq:lower`, `eq:upper` the full triangles are odd in number, hence not zero. Colour classes `{0}`, `{1}`, `{≥ 2}`, so no range premise. General `d` remains open. |

An injection of `d` indices into `m` values gives `d ≤ m`, with the explicit first repeated pair when `m < d`; `d`, `m` and the parameter are internal. |

The colouring `eq:color` is an actual PR symbol; its admissibility and the absence of full triangles are derived, and the contradiction is between two values of the same count. The rational-to-natural reduction (`d ≥ 3` is `DimensionObstruction`), `thm:alternative`, `thm:collision`, `thm:schema` and `cor:dimension` remain open. |

The occupancy colouring is admissible, so `SpernerCube` gives an odd count of full simplices, and no simplex is full (staggered grids and pigeonhole). The rational version is `DimensionRational.rat_obstruction`; `thm:alternative` (witness extraction) remains open. |

The reduction to natural units is the table `⌊dF/h⌋` with a common offset bounding the negative parts (a grid sum). |

The packet itself uses only this rational version ("If uniformity is also specified on all constructive real inputs, the same estimate extends to them by approximation. Only the rational version is used in the proof."); the optional extension to real inputs and the rational affine rescaling of an `n`-cube of `ℝ^n` onto `C_n` are not formalized. |
| Dimension invariance, `thm:alternative` | `DimensionAlternative.alternative` | For every `d > m`, every rational PR table and rational `h`: a fixed finite family of least-witness searches over digit codes (`Foundations/DigitCodes`) always returns a witness checked by natural-number arithmetic, either an `h`-close pair on opposite faces of some coordinate or two vertices of a Kuhn simplex at distance `≥ h/d` in some entry; the closed iPRA sentence is the finite disjunction over the family. |
| Dimension invariance, `thm:collision` | `DimensionCollision.collision` | For every `D > m` and PR `A, μ` with `eq:U`: the fixed searches on the sampled table return, for every `a`, a coordinate `g` and grid points `u ∈ L_g`, `v ∈ U_g` with `dist(A(u/N, p), A(v/N, p)) + 2^{1−p} < 2^{−a}` (the arithmetic form; with `eq:eval` this is `‖f(q) − f(q')‖ < 2^{−a}`). |

The declarations belong to the corresponding namespaces under
`LeanProofs.IPRA.Combinatorics`, `LeanProofs.IPRA.Analysis`, and
`LeanProofs.IPRA.Foundations`. The module links in the table below locate
their full formulas and proofs.

The Barnes packet still requires the analytic canonical-product construction,
complex functional and reflection identities, multiplication formulas,
analytic special values, asymptotic estimates and characterizations. Its
integer product results provide a finite part of those arguments. The FIBER
packet still requires the remaining source-node compiler adapters and cyclic unrolling, cluster and finite-algebra
observation proofs, concrete application instances, and the adapters recorded
in its claim audit. The full source scope remains active.


## Source statements needing correction

- In finite-use-v2 `thm:nested-name`, nesting and shrinking diameters give
  a regular center name. Equality with a particular admissible solution also
  needs its membership in every isolating region, or a theorem locating it
  there. For example, the target set `{0, 1/8}` and balls centered at zero
  of radius `2^(-k-2)` meet the displayed nesting, nonemptiness and local
  diameter conditions. Both solutions belong to the initial ball. The
  solution `1/8` lies outside later balls and differs from the center name.
  The source's global assertion needs that extra compatibility hypothesis.
  [Source statement](../docs/proofs/foundations/ipra_finite_use_framework_2026-11-15_v2/sections/09_compactness.tex)
- In Barnes `eq:product-cutoff`, the displayed cutoff using
  `ceil(2^(p+5) R³ / 3)` yields `B_N ≤ 2^(-p-4)` for
  `B_N = 2 R³ / (3N)`. The following sentence claims `2^(-p-5)`.
  At `R = 1`, `p = 0`, the displayed cutoff is `N = 11`, and
  `B_N = 2/33 > 1/32`. Using `2^(p+6)` in the cutoff supplies the claimed
  bound. The underlying `eq:qtail` bound has its stated constant.
  [Source cutoff](../docs/proofs/analysis/barnes_g_ipra_2026-11-16/paper/sections/03_canonical_product.tex)

- In FIBER Section 8, “Gap-guarded labeling,” a positive distance gap
  between complex-root clusters does not make lexicographic box order
  independent of the certificate. The roots `i` and `−i` have gap `2`;
  arbitrarily small rational boxes containing them can have their real
  coordinates perturbed in either order. Real-coordinate ties require an
  additional order-separation or locator convention. Metric separation alone
  does give unique geometric matching at sufficiently fine precision.
  [Source proposition](../docs/proofs/foundations/fiber_ipra_2026-11-16/paper/sections/08_algebra.tex)

## Packet inventory

The inventory contains 155 packet entries, including all 115 original entries.
The derivations README links proof families to the source packets.
Completion requires reconciling all theorems within each packet, including
claims beyond those families.

`Geometry/MorleyCertificate` and the Type 15 part of `Geometry/PentagonLedger` currently provide symbolic polynomial certificates, not non-vacuous encoded geometry. Their ordinary iPRA formulas use integer variables, and antecedents such as `2*u_c=1`, `rho^2=3` or `h^2=3` have no integer instance. Scalar-quotient or quadratic-ring realizations and the original geometry checkers remain open. The conditional integer ledgers and area identities retain their stated finite algebraic scope.

F02 (for coded topology tables), F08 and F24 are in [Topology/FiniteRecovery](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/FiniteRecovery.lean). In the algebraic section, A03 (finite Euler–Poincaré, in every commutative ring and sign-free for iPRA terms), A06 (Euler additivity) and the Euler half of A05 (collapse) are in [Topology/EulerCharacteristic](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/EulerCharacteristic.lean). A01 (`∂² = 0`, by the sign-reversing pairing of deletion orders) is in [Topology/BoundarySquare](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/BoundarySquare.lean). A09 and A10 (greedy and two-colourings, the odd-cycle certificate) are in [Topology/TwoColoring](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/TwoColoring.lean), A12 and A14 for function sheaves in [Topology/FiniteSheaves](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/FiniteSheaves.lean), A11 (the canonical-cover test), A13, A14 for arbitrary sheaves, A15 (stalkwise isomorphisms) and A16 (local subobjects and equalizers) for arbitrary presheaf tables in [Topology/FiniteSheafTheory](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/FiniteSheafTheory.lean), A17 and A18 in [Topology/BooleanAtoms](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/BooleanAtoms.lean) (these four for coded finite data), and the cone identity of A20 in [Topology/SimplicialCone](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/SimplicialCone.lean). D02 (cubical Sperner parity, the boundary rules `v_i = 0 → λ(v) < i`, `v_i = N → λ(v) ≠ i − 1`) is `sperner_cube` of [Topology/SpernerCube](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/SpernerCube.lean) for every dimension, and D03 (separated threshold arithmetic: two grids of different shifts do not both have a boundary in one unit interval) is [Topology/StaggeredGrids](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/StaggeredGrids.lean), both derived for `dimension_invariance_ipra` (checked against the statements). The finite cores of the remaining metric results (`thm:finite-metric-product`, `thm:product-completion`, `prop:image-nets`, `cor:finite-metric-urysohn`) are in [Topology/MetricNets](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/MetricNets.lean): the maximum metric and the mixed-radix index of the product net, the coordinate Cauchy modulus and the product convergence rate, the image net, and the `1`-Lipschitz minima and the gap of the finite Urysohn data. S04 (`thm:antipodal-finite`) is assembled from A19, S01 and S03 in [Topology/AntipodalObstruction](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/AntipodalObstruction.lean), for a cycle with supplied carrier degree and degenerate prism images; the degeneracy follows from a repeated vertex by [Topology/DegenerateBoundary](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/DegenerateBoundary.lean), and the version with `T` time layers is `finite_obstruction`. S05 and S06 are derived at the level of their finite estimates; their assembly (the labelled time tables and the bounded scan) is not yet derived. The carrier homotopy of A20 and S03 (carrier degree one, for a cycle on which the homotopy vanishes in the top degree) are in [Topology/CarrierHomotopy](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/CarrierHomotopy.lean), for free augmented chain groups with carriers and contracting cones. A19 (the prism identity `∂P + P∂ = g_# − f_#`) is in [Topology/PrismIdentity](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/PrismIdentity.lean). A02 (Betti numbers `b_k = c_k − r_k − r_{k+1}` and `H_k ≅ ℚ^{b_k}`), the rank count of A04 (chain-homotopy inverse maps preserve Betti numbers), the Betti half of A05 (collapse certificates) and A08 (the polygon genera) are in [Topology/RationalHomology](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/RationalHomology.lean), and A07 (graph Betti numbers `b_0 = c`, `b_1 = e − v + c`) in [Topology/GraphBetti](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/GraphBetti.lean). The finite estimates of S04–S06 (the S04 chain argument after the carrier lemma, the S05 labels, and the S06 scan, norm, Lipschitz and normalization bounds) are iPRA derivations over integer terms in [Topology/SphereEstimates](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/SphereEstimates.lean), and L03, L04 also have iPRA forms over integer terms in [Topology/LimitCertificates](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/LimitCertificates.lean) (`center_interior_ipra`, `dual_bound_ipra`, `selected_vanish_ipra`). L01, L02 and L05 are in [Topology/FiniteLimits](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/FiniteLimits.lean), and L03 (the interior step of convex loops), L04 (the rational disk-intersection certificate) and L06 (an exact union extractor would decide halting) are in [Topology/LimitCertificates](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/LimitCertificates.lean), so all of L01–L06 are derived. In the metric section, the geometric tail and coordinate bounds of the weighted product metric (`eq:product-tail`, `eq:coordinate-bound`, the net error of `thm:countable-net-product`) and the local-gap Urysohn estimate (`prop:local-urysohn`) are in [Topology/MetricProducts](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/MetricProducts.lean), cleared of dyadic denominators. The approximate extrema of `cor:approximate-extrema` and the upper endpoint of the certified interval of `thm:computable-uniform-metric` are in [Topology/ApproxExtrema](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/ApproxExtrema.lean). The explicit Hilbert-cube embedding `thm:dense-hilbert-embedding` (truncated coordinates are `1`-Lipschitz, the embedding is `1`-Lipschitz for the weighted metric, and the inverse local modulus `eq:dense-embedding-inverse`) is in [Topology/HilbertEmbedding](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/HilbertEmbedding.lean). The finite cores of the compact-open sandwich `eq:compact-open-sandwich`, evaluation `prop:metric-evaluation`, the converse of `thm:metric-currying` and the inverse neighbourhood transformation of `thm:coordinate-metrization` are in [Topology/MetricNeighborhoods](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/MetricNeighborhoods.lean). `prop:euclidean-open-cube` (`h(t) = t/(1+|t|)` is `1`-Lipschitz, `h⁻¹` is `δ^{−2}`-Lipschitz on `|s| ≤ 1 − δ`, and the maps are inverse) is derived in [Topology/OpenCubeHomeo](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/OpenCubeHomeo.lean). S01 (the cross-polytope cycle: `∂ z_n = 0`, `A_# z_n = (−1)^{n+1} z_n`, `z_n ≠ 0`) is in [Topology/SphereCycle](Lean/IPRADerivations/Topology/SphereCycle.lean). |

The finite laws of conditional averaging (expectation, the defining identity, measurable fixed points, the tower law and the `d₁` contraction, in cleared block-sum form) are derived in [Probability/ConditionalAveraging](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/ConditionalAveraging.lean). The finite inequalities of the represented expectation (the `d₁` contraction of `I`, regularity of `I x_k` for regular names, the bounded Lipschitz test-function bound and the pointwise tightness step) are derived in [Probability/RepresentedExpectation](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/RepresentedExpectation.lean). The finite estimates of the uniform law and the Cantor law of `foundation.tex` (the Cauchy rate of the dyadic midpoints, the ternary tail `3^{-n}` and the covering rate `(2/3)^{3k} ≤ 2^{-k}`) are in [Probability/UniformCantor](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/UniformCantor.lean). The pointwise step and the precision budgets of threshold lifting, the modulus of the effective Vitali completion (from the clipping estimate `clipping` of [Probability/ClipLaws](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/ClipLaws.lean)) and the majorant tail are in [Probability/CompletionRates](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/CompletionRates.lean). The union, intersection and additivity laws of the cylinder algebra (`lem:prob-finite`) are bounded-sum identities in [Probability/CylinderAlgebra](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/CylinderAlgebra.lean). The rational fact behind equality of names, the extension of finite operations to regular names and the row inequality of bounded Lipschitz tests are in [Probability/RegularNames](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/RegularNames.lean). Not derived: the completed order and linearity on names as assembled statements, the passage of the martingale rate to names (its finite block-cleared core `‖C X − X‖₁ ≤ 2‖X − x‖₁` is `martingale_rate` of [Probability/MartingaleRate](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/MartingaleRate.lean), and the bounded stopping theorem `E M_τ = E M_0` of `conditioning.tex` is `optional_stopping` of [Probability/OptionalStopping](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/OptionalStopping.lean)), the kernel-presentation names and moment integrals of the distributions, and the assembly of the histogram sampler and of the coherent `L⁰` realization theorem from their finite budgets (the budgets themselves, `eq:histogram-cdf`, `eq:coupling-clipped` and the Cauchy budget of `thm:density-realization`, are in [Probability/HistogramSampler](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/HistogramSampler.lean)). The exact step-function identity of `lem:monotone-coupling` (the row distance sum equals the threshold sum of CDF differences for nondecreasing tables, with its bound) is in [Probability/MonotoneCoupling](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/MonotoneCoupling.lean), and the finite arithmetic of `distributions.tex` (the normalization Cauchy estimate, the reciprocal bound, the uniform mean and variance identities, the third-moment inequality, and the Gaussian stage exponent) is derived in iPRA in [Probability/DistributionCores](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/DistributionCores.lean). |

The inexact-iteration budget `eq:feedback-inexact` and the finite Neumann producer (residual identity and power bound) are derived in [Analysis/FeedbackInexact](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/FeedbackInexact.lean). In the cluster section, `prop:cluster-polynomial-observations` for nonnegative centers (power sums and elementary symmetric polynomials of matched lists) is derived in [Analysis/ClusterObservations](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/ClusterObservations.lean); the metric estimates of component transport `prop:component-transport` and of the stable locators `thm:stable-locators` (for supplied triangle inequalities) are in [Analysis/ClusterTransport](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/ClusterTransport.lean); the Gaussian-rational form, the breadth-first path construction and the bounded nearest-anchor scan are not derived; `lem:coverage-hall` and `thm:bounded-matching` (a perfect matching or a Hall violator, hence a permutation matching of the enclosures) are in [Analysis/ClusterMatching](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/ClusterMatching.lean). |

The order layer for grid sums is [Foundations/GridSumsOrder](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/GridSumsOrder.lean) (`gs_le_gen`, `Cells`, `gs_const`). Earlier: [Analysis/StokesSlice](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/StokesSlice.lean) (one grid line, the four corner coefficients, the polynomial identity `curl F · (u × v)`). With the bound of the certificate `eq:derivative-cert`, `thm:finite` is `finite_stokes_named` of [StokesNamed](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/StokesNamed.lean): on the scale `S = N · 2^{t+p}` the certificate reads `R_a ≤ C` with `C = 2^p + (2N + 1) 2^t`, and the conclusion `∑_i (B_i − V_i) ≤ ∑_i N^d · C` is `k 2^{-t} + k (2N + 1) 2^{-p}` after division by `S N^{d−1}`; the certificate itself is the hypothesis of the theorem, at the cells of the grid. Not derived: the precision schedule, the rates `thm:rates`, the curved cell `thm:curved`, the chain version and the equality of names. |

| [analytic-number-theory/bertrand_ipra](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/bertrand_ipra/) | **Bertrand's postulate is derived as a closed sentence of iPRA**: `bertrand : [] ⊢ sentence` in [AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandPostulate](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandPostulate.lean), where `sentence` ([BertrandSearch](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandSearch.lean)) says: for all `x ≥ 4`, the least prime `p(x)` found by the bounded search above `x` satisfies `x < p(x)`, `prime p(x)` and `p(x) < 2x − 2`. The route is the packet's (Erdős). (1) `C(2k + 1, k) ≤ 4^k` ([BertrandCentral](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandCentral.lean)), through `C(2k + 2, k + 1) = 2 C(2k + 1, k) ≤ 2 · 4^k`. (2) `∏_{p ≤ r} p ≤ 4^r` ([PrimorialUpper](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PrimorialUpper.lean)) by strong induction, from the one divisibility `∏_{p ≤ 2k+2} p ∣ ∏_{p ≤ k+1} p · C(2k + 1, k)`, derived by comparing valuations at every prime. (3) A prime `q` with `2k/3 < q ≤ k` and `q² > 2k` does not divide `C(2k, k)` ([BertrandMiddle](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandMiddle.lean)), from Legendre's formula. (4) After a failed search, `C(2x, x) ∣ ∏_{i ≤ s} i^{[i prime] v_i(lcm(1..2x))} · ∏_{p ≤ m} p · (2x − 1)` and hence `C(2x, x) ≤ (2x)^{s+1} 4^m` with `s = ⌊√(2x)⌋`, `m = ⌊2x/3⌋` ([BertrandFactor](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandFactor.lean)); the table of prime ranges of the paper is the case analysis of the valuation comparison. (5) The integer inequality: `(d + 39)^6 ≤ 2^{d+36}`, cubing, and the chain of powers refute `4^x ≤ (2x + 1)^{s+2} 4^m` for `s ≥ 38` ([BertrandGrowth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandGrowth.lean)). (6) The finite certificate: the ten primes `5, 7, 11, 19, 31, 59, 113, 223, 443, 883`, each derived prime in iPRA by trial division ([Foundations/NumeralPrimes](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/NumeralPrimes.lean)), cover `4 ≤ x ≤ 882` ([BertrandCertificate](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BertrandCertificate.lean)). Deviations from the paper: the factorization (7) of `C(2n, n)` and the splitting of products by ranges are replaced by divisibilities obtained from valuations; the split between the certificate and the inequality is at `882` (the paper: `722`); the step of (10) is a polynomial inequality with nonnegative coefficients. Not restated: the leastness of `p(x)` (`before_witness` of BertrandSearch) and the initial values `p(1), p(2), p(3)`. |

| [analytic-number-theory/mertens-ipra](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/mertens-ipra/) | The Mertens function is the pair `M = M⁺ − M⁻` of the summatory functions of `μ^±` (`MP`, `MN` of [MobiusFloor](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusFloor.lean)). Derived from `identities.tex`: `lem:weightedsum` for any weight and any body (`weighted_floor` of [MertensTotient](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MertensTotient.lean), which is `ConvSummatory.conv_summatory`); both identities of `thm:floor-recurrence`: `∑_{a ≤ N} M(⌊N/a⌋) = 1` (`mertens_floor`, valid at `N = 0` with `[N ≥ 1]` on the right) and `M(N) = 1 − ∑_{a=2}^{N} M(⌊N/a⌋)` for `N ≥ 1` (`mertens_recursion` of [MertensRecursion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MertensRecursion.lean)); the first identity of `thm:totient-chebyshev`, `∑_{a ≤ N} a M(⌊N/a⌋) = ∑_{m ≤ N} φ(m)` (`totient_floor`). From `foundations.tex`, `lem:cancel` is `mobius_cancel`, `mobius_one` of [Mobius](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Mobius.lean), and as an identity of convolutions `unit_pt` of [MobiusInversionReverse](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusInversionReverse.lean). The Chebyshev identity (`thm:totient-chebyshev`, second identity) is derived for every weight `w` in place of the logarithm: `∑_{a ≤ x} L_w(a) M⁺(⌊x/a⌋) = ψ_w(x) + ∑_{a ≤ x} L_w(a) M⁻(⌊x/a⌋)` (`chebyshev_mertens` of [MertensChebyshev](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MertensChebyshev.lean), with the floor form `chebyshev_floor`: `ψ(x) = ∑_d μ(d) log(⌊x/d⌋!)`). The quotient blocks of `thm:floor-recurrence` are `block_of_quo`, `quo_of_block` of [QuotientBlocks](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/QuotientBlocks.lean) (`⌊N/a⌋ = q` exactly when `⌊N/(q + 1)⌋ < a ≤ ⌊N/q⌋`), with their number `block_count` (at most `2⌊√N⌋` changes of the quotient). The uniqueness assertion of `thm:floor-recurrence` is derived for signed tables `g = g⁺ − g⁻` with natural parts: if `∑_{a ≤ s} g(⌊s/a⌋) = [s ≥ 1]` for all `s ≤ X` (one quantifier-free equation, the vanishing of a bounded sum of errors), then `g⁺(t) + M⁻(t) = g⁻(t) + M⁺(t)` for `1 ≤ t ≤ X` (`mertens_unique` of [MertensUnique](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MertensUnique.lean), by induction on `t` on the count of disagreements below `t`; `mertens_err`: the Mertens function satisfies the hypothesis). The product form `∏_{a=1}^{N} a^{M(⌊N/a⌋)} = lcm(1, …, N)` is derived as the natural-number equality `∏ a^{M⁺(⌊N/a⌋)} = lcm(1, …, N) · ∏ a^{M⁻(⌊N/a⌋)}` (`mertens_lcm_product` of [MertensLcm](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MertensLcm.lean)): the Chebyshev identity at the weight `[q = p]` compares the valuations at every prime `p`, and positive numbers with equal valuations are equal (`val_ext` of [ValuationExt](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ValuationExt.lean)). Not derived: `thm:hyperboloid`, `thm:redheffer`, `thm:farey`, and the chapters `growth.tex`, `analytic.tex`, `cancellation.tex`, `oscillation.tex`, `rates.tex`, `reverse.tex`, `supplements.tex` and `computation.tex`. |
| [analytic-number-theory/mobius_ipra](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/mobius_ipra/) | Parts of `arithmetic.tex` are already derived by modules written for other packets, with `μ = μ⁺ − μ⁻` a pair of natural-number bodies. "Divisor cancellation", first identity `∑_{d ∣ n} μ(d) = ε(n)`: `mobius_cancel` (`n ≥ 2`) and `mobius_one` of [Mobius](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Mobius.lean). "Two-sided inversion", the direction `F = f * 1 ⇒ f = μ * F`: `mobius_inversion` of [MobiusInversion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusInversion.lean), with a packed parameter in [MobiusInversionP](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusInversionP.lean) and summed over `n ≤ x` in [MobiusSummatory](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusSummatory.lean) (`mobius_summatory`); the consequence `φ(n) = ∑_{d ∣ n} μ(d) n/d` is `phi_mobius`. The reverse direction `f = μ * F ⇒ F = f * 𝟏` is derived for every body `F` (`mobius_reverse`, `inversion_reverse` of [MobiusInversionReverse](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusInversionReverse.lean), with the unit law `conv_unit`, distributivity `conv_add_l`, `conv_add_r`, and the cancellation as an identity of convolutions at every argument, `unit_pt`), and so are the identities `1 = ∑_{d ≤ N} μ(d) ⌊N/d⌋ = ∑_{k ≤ N} M(⌊N/k⌋)` for every `N` in the form with `[N ≥ 1]` on the right (`mobius_floor`, `mertens_floor` of [MobiusFloor](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusFloor.lean)). Not derived: `∑_{d ∣ n} |μ(d)| = 2^{ω(n)}` and the count of signs, the coded-group form of the inversion, the squarefree count as stated, and the theorems of `boundary.tex`, `equivalence.tex`, `analytic.tex` and `bootstrap.tex`. |

| [analytic-number-theory/pnt_li_ipra_2026-11-15](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/pnt_li_ipra_2026-11-15/) | [AnalyticNumberTheory/DyadicModulus](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DyadicModulus.lean) |
| [analytic-number-theory/prime-counting-ipra](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/prime-counting-ipra/) | Derived from `sections/foundations.tex`: Theorem "Rank and inverse rank" ([PrimeRank](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PrimeRank.lean): `π(x + 1) = π(x) + [x + 1 prime]`, monotonicity, the `k`-th prime `p_k` as a bounded search below `E(k)` with `E(0) = 1`, `E(k + 1) = E(k)! + 1`, `pk_rank`: `π(p_k) = k` and `p_k` prime for `k ≥ 1`, `pk_pc_le`, `lt_pk_pc`: `p_{π(x)} ≤ x < p_{π(x)+1}`, `pk_of_prime`); the runs of composite numbers `N! + 2, …, N! + N`, on which `π` is constant (`composite_run`, `pc_run` of [PrimeGaps](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PrimeGaps.lean)); and "every prime contributes to exactly one class", `∑_{r < a} π_{a,r}(N) = π(N)` (`class_partition` of [PrimeClasses](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PrimeClasses.lean)). From `sections/growth.tex`, the Proposition "Integer Chebyshev bounds" in both halves: `⌊N/2⌋ ≤ (b + 1) π(N)` for `N < 2^{b+1}` and `b π(N) ≤ 13 N` for `b ≥ 2`, `2^b ≤ N` (`cheb_lower_half`, `cheb_upper_b` of [ChebyshevUpper](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ChebyshevUpper.lean), with the general `cheb_upper_gen`: `L π(N) ≤ L π(2^L) + 2N` for `2^L ≤ N`), through `lcm(1, …, P) ≤ P^{π(P)}`, `2^{P−1} ≤ (P + 1) P^{π(P)}` and `2^k ≤ C(2k, k) ≤ (2k)^{π(2k)}` (`lcm_le_pi`, `cheb_lower`, `cb_lower`, `cb_le_pi` of [ChebyshevPi](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ChebyshevPi.lean)) and the primes of a window against the primorial (`prime_window`). The arithmetic prime number theorem imported by that section is `arith_pnt` of [FinalPNT](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/FinalPNT.lean). The modulus of `π(n)/n → 0` is `pi_density` of [ChebyshevCorollaries](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ChebyshevCorollaries.lean) (`k π(N) < N` for `N ≥ 2^b`, `b ≥ 13k + 1`). Golomb's theorem with a bounded witness is `golomb` of [GolombSolution](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/GolombSolution.lean): for `k ≥ 2` the least `N ≥ 2` with `k π(N) ≤ N` satisfies `N = k π(N)` and `N ≤ 2^{13k+1} + 1` (the bound comes from `pi_density`, not from the packet's `B(k)`). Legendre's identity and the sieve recursion are derived with the list of the first primes replaced by a modulus, `Φ(x; P) = #{1 ≤ m ≤ x : gcd(m, P) = 1}`: the inclusion-exclusion formula `∑_{d ≤ x} [d ∣ P] μ⁺(d) ⌊x/d⌋ = Φ(x; P) + ∑_{d ≤ x} [d ∣ P] μ⁻(d) ⌊x/d⌋` for every `P` (`legendre_sieve` of [LegendreSieve](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LegendreSieve.lean), through `((f · [· ∣ P]) * 𝟏)(m) = (f * 𝟏)(gcd(m, P))`); `Φ(x; P(z)) + π(z) = 1 + π(x)` for the primorial `P(z)`, `1 ≤ x`, `z ≤ x < (z + 1)²`, in particular at `z = ⌊√x⌋` (`sieve_pi`, `sieve_pi_sqrt`, `legendre_pi` of [SieveSurvivors](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveSurvivors.lean)); and `Φ(x; P) = Φ(x; P p) + Φ(⌊x/p⌋; P)` for a prime `p` coprime to `P`, with its form over the primorials (`recursion`, `phi_step` of [SieveRecursion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveRecursion.lean)), and the period `Φ(x + P; P) = Φ(x; P) + φ(P)` (`phi_period` of [SievePeriod](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SievePeriod.lean)). Not derived: the remaining congruence-class statements, the rest of the sieve section (the marking loop, the binary-mask form and the floor bound of the inclusion-exclusion formula, Meissel, Lehmer, the count of all Golomb solutions), and the sections `exact.tex`, `chebsharp.tex`, `li.tex`, `riemannR.tex`, `nthfinite.tex`, `complex.tex`, `conditional.tex` and `numerics.tex`. |
| [analytic-number-theory/prime_distribution_ipra](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/prime_distribution_ipra/) | [Foundations/PrecisionArithmetic](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/PrecisionArithmetic.lean), [Foundations/LeastBoundedWitness](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/LeastBoundedWitness.lean); the elimination of a vanishing rational error and the bounded quantifier bits with least search are checked; the weighted Vaughan identity is derived (`vaughan` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/VaughanIdentity](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/VaughanIdentity.lean), for any term `U` and any cutoff body `V(N)`), as is the rational Gram certificate (`gram_bound` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/GramBound](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/GramBound.lean), with its sum-of-squares steps `gauss_cs`, `pair_bound` in [AnalyticNumberTheory/GramCertificate](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/GramCertificate.lean)); for the sieve inequality `thm:finite-sieve` the diagonalization `eq:sieve-diagonal` is derived in bilinear form for natural bodies (`gcd_phi_sum` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/GcdPhiSum](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/GcdPhiSum.lean): `gcd(d, e) = Σ_{r ≤ Z} [r ∣ d][r ∣ e] φ(r)`; `diag`, `diag_term` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveDiagonal](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveDiagonal.lean): `Σ_{1 ≤ d ≤ Z} Σ_{e<N} u_d v_e gcd(d, e) = Σ_{r ≤ Z} φ(r) (Σ_{1 ≤ d ≤ Z, r ∣ d} u_d)(Σ_{e<N, r ∣ e} v_e)`); the inversion `eq:sieve-inversion` is derived as the Möbius inversion over multiples for natural bodies (`dual_inversion` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveInversion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveInversion.lean)), and the counting step `eq:crt-count` for `q ≥ 1` as the two cleared inequalities `q l · C(X) ≤ X + q l` and `X ≤ q l · C(X) + q l` (`count_le`, `count_ge` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveCount](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveCount.lean)); the expansion of the square over the progression is derived for natural bodies (`square_exchange` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveSquare](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveSquare.lean), by two uses of `div_exchange` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/DivisorExchange](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DivisorExchange.lean)), and the count for a pair `(d, e)` at `l = [d, e]` in the form `q d e · K ≤ gcd(d, e) · X + q d e`, `gcd(d, e) · X ≤ q d e · K + q d e` (`pair_le`, `pair_ge` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/SievePairCount](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SievePairCount.lean)); the sieve inequality before the choice of weights is derived for arbitrary signed weights `λ = λ⁺ − λ⁻` with natural parts, `q ≥ 1`, `gcd(a, q) = 1` and `Z ≥ 2`: `q (λ_1)² S ≤ X Σ λ_d λ_e / [d, e] + q (Σ |λ_d|)²`, cleared of signs (`sieve_ineq` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveInequality](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveInequality.lean)), where `S` counts the `m < X` of the progression with no divisor in `[2, Z)`. It combines the summed majorant `eq:sieve-majorant` (`majorant` of [SieveMajorant](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveMajorant.lean), with [SieveRough](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveRough.lean)) and the bilinear estimate `|q N − X G| ≤ q L L'` (`bil_le`, `bil_ge` of [SieveClosedForms](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveClosedForms.lean), through [SievePairSum](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SievePairSum.lean), [SieveExpansion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveExpansion.lean) and [SieveBilinear](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveBilinear.lean)). The passage from `S` to the prime count is derived: `π(x; q, a) ≤ S + Z` (`primes_le` of [SievePrimes](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SievePrimes.lean)), hence the same inequality with `π(x; q, a) − Z` in place of `S` (`prime_sieve_ineq` of [SievePrimeInequality](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SievePrimeInequality.lean)), still for arbitrary weights. For the explicit weights `eq:explicit-sieve-weights`, with the denominators cleared by `z!` (`phi_quo` of [SieveWeightFacts](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightFacts.lean)) and `A = z! G = Σ_{r ≤ z} μ(r)² [gcd(r, q) = 1] z!/φ(r)`, the value of the quadratic form is derived for `q ≥ 1`: the signed form equals `z! · A`, which is `Q(λ) = 1/G` of `eq:sieve-normalization` after division by `A²` (`form_value` of [SieveWeightForm](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightForm.lean), through [SieveWeightRows](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightRows.lean) and [SieveWeightValues](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightValues.lean)). `λ_1 = 1` is derived as `λ⁺_1 = A`, `λ⁻_1 = 0` (`lam_P`, `lam_N` of [SieveWeightOne](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightOne.lean)), and the weight bound `eq:weight-bound` as `D · W_D ≤ A` for every `D` and `Σ_{d ≤ z} (λ⁺_d + λ⁻_d) ≤ z · A` (`point_bound`, `weight_bound` of [SieveWeightBound](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightBound.lean)). The route to `|λ_d| ≤ 1` differs from the paper: instead of the closed form `eq:weight-formula` and the injection `(e, s) ↦ es` over all divisors, it is a strong induction on `d` through its least prime factor ([Foundations/StrongInduction](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/StrongInduction.lean)), with the one-prime step `p · W_{Dp} ≤ W_D` (`prime_step` of [SieveWeightStep](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightStep.lean), from `(p − 1) |Y_{kp}| ≤ |Y_k|` of [SieveWeightPrime](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightPrime.lean)) and the triangle inequality for the inversion ([SieveWeightAbs](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveWeightAbs.lean)). The first inequality of `thm:finite-sieve` follows for `q ≥ 1`, `gcd(a, q) = 1`, `z ≥ 1`: `q · A · π(X; q, a) ≤ X · z! + q · A · (z² + z)` with `A = z! G_q(z)` (`finite_sieve` of [FiniteSieve](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/FiniteSieve.lean), assembled in [SieveFinite](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveFinite.lean), with the count `π ≤ S + z` of [SievePrimesSharp](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SievePrimesSharp.lean)). For the denominator bound `eq:sieve-denominator` the prime step of the comparison `H ≤ G` is derived: with `S_a(t) = ∑_{m ≤ t} [m ∣ a^L] ⌊z!/m⌋` and `G_a(t) = ∑_{d ≤ t} [d ∣ a] ⌊z!/φ(d)⌋`, for a prime `p` with `gcd(p, a) = 1` and `t ≤ z`, `S_a(t) ≤ G_a(t)` and `S_{ap}(⌊t/p⌋) ≤ G_{ap}(⌊t/p⌋)` give `S_{ap}(t) ≤ G_{ap}(t)` (`prime_step` of [DenominatorPrimeStep](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DenominatorPrimeStep.lean)), from `p S_{ap}(N) ≤ p S_a(N) + S_{ap}(⌈N/p⌉)` (`trunc_step` of [SmoothTruncated](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SmoothTruncated.lean)), `g_{ap}(d) = g_a(d) + [p ∣ d] g_{ap}(d)` and `(p − 1) g_{ap}(p k) = g_a(k)` (`split_pt`, `scale_pt` of [TotientTermStep](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/TotientTermStep.lean)), and their sums `G_{ap}(t) = G_a(t) + W(t)`, `G_a(⌊t/p⌋) = (p − 1) W(t)` (`G_split`, `W_scale` of [TotientSumStep](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/TotientSumStep.lean)). The step is iterated: `C_z(a) = ∑_{t ≤ z} (S_a(t) ∸ G_a(t)) = 0` gives `C_z(a p) = 0` (`range_step` of [DenominatorRange](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DenominatorRange.lean), a course-of-values induction on the range), and over the primes coprime to `q`: for `A_q(y) = ∏_{p ≤ y, gcd(p, q) = 1} p`, `C_z(A_q(y)) = 0` for every `y`, that is `∑_{m ≤ t} [m ∣ A_q(y)^L] ⌊z!/m⌋ ≤ ∑_{d ≤ t} [d ∣ A_q(y)] ⌊z!/φ(d)⌋` for `t ≤ z` (`Cz_all`, `smooth_le_totient` of [DenominatorModulus](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DenominatorModulus.lean)). At `y = t = z` both sides are identified: every `m ≤ z` coprime to `q` divides `A_q(z)^z` (`dvd_Aq_pow`, `Hq_le_G` of [DenominatorSmooth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DenominatorSmooth.lean)), and a divisor of `A_q(z)` is squarefree and coprime to `q` (`sq_zero_of_dvd`, `cop_of_dvd`), so its term is a term of the denominator `A = z! G_q(z)`: `∑_{1 ≤ m ≤ z, gcd(m, q) = 1} ⌊z!/m⌋ ≤ z! G_q(z)`, that is `H_q(z) ≤ G_q(z)` (`Hq_le_Asum` of [DenominatorSquarefree](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DenominatorSquarefree.lean)). The factor `q/φ(q)`: for a prime `p`, `p · H^{(a)}(N) ≤ p · H^{(ap)}(N) + H^{(a)}(N)` (`harm_step` of [DenominatorBound](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DenominatorBound.lean): the terms with `p ∣ m` are reindexed by `m = p k`, and `p ⌊F/(pk)⌋ ≤ ⌊F/k⌋`), hence `φ(q) · H^{(1)}(N) ≤ q · H^{(q)}(N)` for `q ≥ 1` by strong induction on `q` through its least prime factor (`harm_factor`; the paper's product of finite geometric sums over the primes of `q` is not used). Together: `φ(q) · ∑_{1 ≤ m ≤ z} ⌊z!/m⌋ ≤ q · z! G_q(z)`, that is `G_q(z) ≥ (φ(q)/q) H_z` (`sieve_denominator`, `eq:sieve-denominator`), and the second inequality of `eq:finite-sieve` for `q ≥ 1`: `φ(q) H · π(X; q, a) ≤ X z! + φ(q) H (z² + z)` with `H = z! H_z` (`finite_sieve_second`). The modulus is any `q ≥ 1`, as in `thm:finite-sieve`: the residue count has one element per period for `q = 1` directly (`period_one` of [SieveCount](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveCount.lean)), and the closure of the units modulo `q` under products and factors is derived for `q ≥ 1` in [CoprimeModulus](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/CoprimeModulus.lean). Not derived: the Brun-Titchmarsh theorems and the Dirichlet bound. |
| [analytic-number-theory/prime_reciprocal_ipra_2026-11-15](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/prime_reciprocal_ipra_2026-11-15/) | [AnalyticNumberTheory/BinomialThree](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/BinomialThree.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/Harmonic](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Harmonic.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/LocalFactor](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LocalFactor.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerCleared](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerCleared.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/SumReindex](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SumReindex.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/Smooth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Smooth.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/EulerDomination](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/EulerDomination.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/FactorVector](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/FactorVector.lean) |
| [analytic-number-theory/riemann_hypotheses_ipra_2026-11-20](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/riemann_hypotheses_ipra_2026-11-20/) | [AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann/) (the packet Lean imported into the shared 50-module Riemann library). The three closed sentences RH, GRH, ERH (`Hypotheses`); in object iPRA ERH → GRH → RH by specialization (`Hierarchy`, `Hypotheses`) and each sentence ↔ the absence of a failing tuple (`RHCounterexamples`, `RiemannIPRA`); the standard reading and soundness at ℕ (`StandardReading`). The correspondence with critical-strip zeros is classical mathematics, not an iPRA derivation. |
| [analytic-number-theory/riemann_ipra_equivalences_2026-11-20](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/riemann_ipra_equivalences_2026-11-20/) | [AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann/) (imported packet Lean). Object-iPRA biconditionals: first failure, failure counts, eventual/cofinal cleanliness and fixed PR sampling (`ObjectForms`), exact slack and slack certificates (`SlackForms`), `prog_grh_to_rh` (`SplitPrimes`), `dedekind_to_dirichlet`, `dirichlet_to_riemann` (`PrimeForms`), the Lagarias counterexample form (`Lagarias`). Standard-model and finite-algebra parts (18 discrete readings, the determinant identity `thm:matrix` as an integer-matrix identity, Farey/energy estimates, Abel identity) are Lean metatheory; `thm:rate` is a primitive-recursive witness transformation and is being derived: (2) ⇒ (1) ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RateAbel](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RateAbel.lean) `recover`, with `u_N S_r(N)` cleared as a primitive recursion) and (1) ⇒ (2) ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RateShell](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RateShell.lean) `w_all`, the shell majorants as an integer invariant) are done, and [AnalyticNumberTheory/RateIff](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RateIff.lean) `rate_iff` proves (1) ↔ (2) in object iPRA for signed coefficient bodies, and [AnalyticNumberTheory/RateCauchy](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RateCauchy.lean) / [AnalyticNumberTheory/RateCauchyIff](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RateCauchyIff.lean) `rate3_iff` proves (1) ↔ (3) with the packet's explicit Cauchy modulus: `thm:rate` is complete. The analytic links (Mellin transforms, zero theory) are classical and not iPRA derivations. `thm:energy`: the recovery direction and its witness conversion are derived in object iPRA for every signed PR sequence `M = M⁺ − M⁻` given by two bodies: `d² ≤ 4J₂(d)` by the union bound ([AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyJordan](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyJordan.lean) `jordan_quarter`), the cleared divisor inversion `Σ_{d≤N} μ(d) T'_d = N! M(N)` with the two signs of `T'_d = N! T_d` accumulated separately ([AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyMobius](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyMobius.lean) `mobius_energy`), the cleared weighted Cauchy–Schwarz `(N!)² M(N)² ≤ 8 Q_N` with `Q_N = (N!)² E_N` ([Analysis/WeightedCauchySchwarz](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/WeightedCauchySchwarz.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyRecovery](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyRecovery.lean) `energy_recovery`), and the conversion "energy bound `c` ⇒ `8^r c` for `M`" ([AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyWitness](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/EnergyWitness.lean) `energy_witness`). Open for `thm:energy`: the forward dyadic-block estimate (a bound `c` for `M` gives `(512r)^r (c+1)` for the energy), and the link of the bodies `QN` to the packet's PR programs in `FareyEnergy.lean`. |
| [analytic-number-theory/riemann_ipra_formalization_2026-11-20](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/riemann_ipra_formalization_2026-11-20/) | [AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann/) (imported packet Lean). Object-iPRA derivations: countdown and precision-budget changes of the terminal zero test (`CountdownTests`, `PrecisionBounds`), one-sided rotating sampling (`OneSided`), Cayley and Hardy–Toeplitz scheduled tests, Robin/Nicolas first-failure forms (`RobinNicolas`), the scheduled universal criteria (`UniversalCriteria`), the product certificate (`ProductCertificate`). Residue-space / Gram elimination, grid interpolation and strip reflection are Lean metatheory; the zero theorems behind the standard interpretations are classical. |
| [analytic-number-theory/riemann_ipra_prime_criteria_2026-11-20](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/riemann_ipra_prime_criteria_2026-11-20/) | [AnalyticNumberTheory/CubeScan](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/CubeScan.lean); the object-iPRA results `thm:cubef` (one finite cube, witness-preserving) and the multiplicative certificate. The classical standard-model equivalences with RH/GRH/ERH (`thm:main`, `thm:split`, `thm:taylor`) are not iPRA derivations; `thm:symmetric-prefix` is derived in object iPRA (exponential, moment and product tests); `thm:powerdivisor`: its witness transfer is derived in object iPRA for arbitrary signed terms ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerBudget](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerBudget.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerTransfer](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerTransfer.lean): `PowerTransfer.transfer`, the packet's `power_divisor_transfer`); both finite identities along perfect powers are derived for arbitrary coefficient bodies ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorPrefix](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorPrefix.lean) `prefix_identity`, [AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorRow](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorRow.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorInversion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorInversion.lean) `inversion`); and the growth equivalence itself is derived: [AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorGrowth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorGrowth.lean) `growth_iff` proves `(∀k ∃c ∀N |A(N)|^{2(k+1)} ≤ cN^{k+2}) ↔ (same for B_h)` in object iPRA for every `h ≥ 2` and arbitrary signed coefficient bodies (so for the RH, cyclotomic and Dedekind rows once their coefficient bodies are supplied), with the packet's witness `C_r(c) = (c+1)(8r)^{2r}`. The single sentence uniform in `h` is derived too ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorUniform](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerDivisorUniform.lean) `growth_uniform_iff`). For the residue-class rows, the residue transport ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidue](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidue.lean)), the progression-row identity `B_{h;q,b}(N) = Σ_d [gcd(d^h,q)=1] A_{q,b_{d^h}}(⌊N/d^h⌋)` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerTwistPrefix](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerTwistPrefix.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueRows](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueRows.lean) `masked_B`), the forward transfer ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueForward](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueForward.lean) `forward_res_core`) and an abstract Möbius inversion from a row identity ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerAbstractInversion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerAbstractInversion.lean) `inversion_g`) are derived, and so are the residue inversion over units ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueInversion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueInversion.lean) `inversion_res`), the backward transfer ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueBackward](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueBackward.lean)) and the sentence-level equivalence ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueGrowth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PowerResidueGrowth.lean) `growth_res_iff`: residue-uniform growth of `A_{q,b}` ↔ that of `B_{h;q,b}`, `q ≥ 2`, witness `C_r(c)`); the `q = 1` row is the ordinary `growth_iff`. Not yet derived: the identification of the packet's compiled coefficient programs with these bodies., [AnalyticNumberTheory/SymmetricPrefix](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SymmetricPrefix.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/SymmetricMoment](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SymmetricMoment.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/SymmetricProduct](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SymmetricProduct.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/Riemann/) (imported packet Lean, incl. `CubeTests`, `SymmetricPrefixes`, `TaylorPackets`, `LiouvilleCriterion`) |
| [analytic-number-theory/robin_ipra_research_2026-11-19](../docs/proofs/analytic-number-theory/robin_ipra_research_2026-11-19/) | [AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogic](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogic.lean); the logical layer (`prop:logic`, `thm:assembly`, the packet Lean shell) in object iPRA. [AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGamma](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGamma.lean) derives the finite telescoping core of `lem:gamma` in object iPRA: with `log(1+1/m)` named by the cleared truncations `S_K`, the increments `δ_K(m)` of `a_m = H_m − log m − 1/(2m)` dominate the explicit truncation tail (`q_inv`), satisfy `δ_K(m) ≤ 1/(12m²) − 1/(12(m+1)²)` for `K ≥ 2` (`p_inv`), and telescope to `Σ_{m₀≤i<M} δ_K(i) ≤ 1/(12m₀²) − 1/(12M²)` (`gamma_tail`); the identification of the limit name with the analytic Euler constant is external. The multiplicativity of `σ` used by the lcm bridge and `thm:three` is derived ([AnalyticNumberTheory/DivisorFactor](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/DivisorFactor.lean) `divisor_factor`: `Σ_{d∣mN} g(d) = Σ_{a∣m}Σ_{b∣N} g(ab)` for coprime `m, N`, and [AnalyticNumberTheory/SigmaMult](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SigmaMult.lean) `sigma_mul`). The prime-power formula `(p−1)σ(p^a) + 1 = p^{a+1}` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/SigmaPrimePower](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SigmaPrimePower.lean)) and the integer half of `thm:three`, `4σ(n) < 15n` for every `n` with at most three distinct prime factors ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinThree](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinThree.lean) `three_core`), are derived, as is the general product formula `σ(n) Π_{p∣n}(p−1) = Π_{p∣n}(p^{v_p(n)+1}−1)` behind both the `thm:three` estimate and the lcm bridge's exact identity ([AnalyticNumberTheory/SigmaProduct](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SigmaProduct.lean) `sigma_product`). The integer half of the lcm bridge's rough Chebyshev bound is derived: `2^{m²−1} ≤ (m²+1)(m²)^m ∏_{p≤m²} p` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/PrimorialLower](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PrimorialLower.lean) `primorial_lower`, from `lcm_primorial` and `two_pow_lcm`). The explicit interval arithmetic is derived on rational names: nested logarithm names for `eq:logtail` with the uniform tail `eq:uniform-log-tail` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogName](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogName.lean)), `LogI(q, k)` with binary range reduction, consistent across precisions and monotone in `q` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogInterval](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogInterval.lean)), the nested exponential names of `eq:exptail` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinExpName](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinExpName.lean)), and the Euler-constant enclosure `γ_lo(t, K) ≤ γ_hi(t, K')` inside `[1/2, 7/12]`, with `γ_lo ≥ 4/7` for `t ≥ 3` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGammaInterval](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGammaInterval.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGammaUpper](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGammaUpper.lean)). `thm:three` is complete in the certificate form `eq:pos`: the interval program `L(n, k)` (double logarithm at precision `r = k + ℓ(n) + 16`, times `E_{K_e}(γ_lo)`) and `Pos(n, k)`, with `B(n) ≥ B(5041) > 15/4` derived on the names by monotonicity and nesting down to three closed cross-multiplications, give `Pos(p^a q^b r^c, k)` for all `k` whenever `p^a q^b r^c ≥ 5041` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinThreePos](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinThreePos.lean) `three_pos`). The upper end `U(n, k)`, `Neg` (`eq:neg`) and the incompatibility of `Pos(n, k)` with `Neg(n, k')` at any two precisions are derived ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinUpperEnd](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinUpperEnd.lean) `lu`, `pos_neg`), as are the width `≤ 2^{-k}` of `LogI(q, k)` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogWidth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogWidth.lean) `logI_width`) and the elementary inequalities `1 − 1/q ≤ log q ≤ q − 1` on the names ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogDiff](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLogDiff.lean)). The packet's schedule is followed except that `γ` telescopes from `a_1 = 1/2` (the construction of `lem:gamma`'s proof) and the `[1/2, 7/12]`, `[1, 3]` intersections are omitted. The rough Chebyshev lemma of the lcm bridge is derived on the logarithm names: `ϑ(m²) ≥ m²/4` for every `m ≥ 16` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeTheta](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeTheta.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeSide](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeSide.lean) `theta_m16`). The finite schedule of the eta checker (Taylor cutoff and error budgets) is derived in [AnalyticNumberTheory/EtaSchedule](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/EtaSchedule.lean); its analytic adequacy (argument principle, completeness) is the packet's own open obligation. The `T` loss is derived in the product form in which it enters the bridge: `∏_{p≤m²}(1 − p^{−a_p−1}) ≥ 1 − 2/m` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeLoss](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeLoss.lean) `loss_sum`, `prod_lower`), so the transfer needs no log-name additivity. The finite counterexample transfer is derived in product form: for `m ≥ 2` and `L = lcm(1, …, m²)`, the name-level hypothesis `∏_{p∣L}(1 − 1/p)·[e^γ log log L]_hi < 1 − 2/m` gives `NegR(L, k)` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeTransfer](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeTransfer.lean) `transfer`, with the exact identity of [AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeExact](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeExact.lean) and `SigmaProduct.sigma_product`). The comparisons of logarithm names of different arguments are derived: Lipschitz bounds `2(z′ − z)(1 − z^{2K})/(1 − z²) ≤ S_K(z′) − S_K(z) ≤ 2(z′ − z)/(1 − z′²)` for the artanh truncations ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipName](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipName.lean); the upper names `U_K` do not satisfy the upper bound, their slope exceeds `2/(1 − z²)`), the two-sided bounds `(x′ − x)/x′ − (x′ − x)/(x 9^K) ≤ lo(x′, K) − lo(x, K) ≤ (x′ − x)/x` for `x ≤ x′ ≤ 2x` across binades ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipArg](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipArg.lean), [AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipLog](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipLog.lean)), and the finitary addition theorem `|lo(xc, K) − lo(x, K) − lo(c, K)| ≤ (c − 1)/9^K` for all rationals `x, c ≥ 1`, by a finite Riemann-sum comparison along `x t_i`, `t_i` with `N = E + 1` steps ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipSum](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipSum.lean) `lo_sub_up`, `lo_sub_low`), with `lo(q) ≤ q − 1`, `lo(4) ≤ 3/2` and `lo(xP^j) ≤ lo(x) + j lo(P) + j(P−1)/9^K` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipBound](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipBound.lean)). The loss `D(m²) ≤ 8/m` is derived in multiplicative form on the names: for `m ≥ 16`, `UH ≤ (1 + 8/m)·H_θ`, where `UH` is the double-logarithm upper name of the Robin upper end `U(L, k)` and `H_θ = hi(log Θ, K_h)` with `Θ = hi(log ∏_{p≤m²} p, K_θ)` an upper name of `ϑ(m²)` at any precisions `K_θ ≥ 2`, `K_h` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeDLoss](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeDLoss.lean) `dloss`, through `ψ̂ ≤ Θ + m·lo(log m²) + 1` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeDPsi](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LcmBridgeDPsi.lean) `psi_le`, i.e. `ψ ≤ ϑ + m log P` on names by the addition theorem). The transfer is thereby restated through `ϑ(m²)`: `∏_{p∣L}(1 − 1/p)·[e^γ]_hi·H_θ·(1 + 8/m) < 1 − 2/m` gives `NegR(L, k)` (`transfer_theta`), and so does the packet form `F_hi(m²) = ∏(1 − 1/p)·[e^γ]_hi·H_θ < 1 − 12/m` (`transfer_theta12`; it gives `−log F(m²) > 12/m`). The composed double-logarithm width is derived on the branch `log n ≥ 1`: `hi(log x′, K′) − lo(log x, K′) ≤ (x′ − x)/x + 2^{−k}` for an enclosure `[x, x′] ⊆ [1, 2x]` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipWidth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinLipWidth.lean) `comp_width`). The composed widths are derived: the Euler-constant enclosure has `γ_hi(t, K) − γ_lo(t, K) ≤ 9t/(4(2K+1)3^{2K+1}) + 1/(12(1+t)²)` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGammaWidth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinGammaWidth.lean) `gamma_width`), the exponential names satisfy `E_{K+1}(u) − E_{K+1}(l) ≤ (u − l)E_K(u) ≤ 3(u − l)` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinExpWidth](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinExpWidth.lean) `exp_lip`), and for every `n ≥ 4` and every `k` the Robin interval has width `U(n, k) − L(n, k) ≤ 2^{−k}` ([AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinWidthCompose](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinWidthCompose.lean) `width`, with the double-logarithm width `UH ≤ LL + 3·2^{−r}` (`w1`), `T ≤ E + 2/(K_e+1)! + 3(γ_hi − γ_lo)`, `LL ≤ n`, and the budget of [AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinWidthNum](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/RobinWidthNum.lean)). For `n = 2` the 2^{−k} bound is false for the program as defined: the lower name of `log 2` lies below `1`, and `L`'s reciprocal branch uses the crude corner `e^γ ≤ 3` (`Lm = cnd·(3·LHn)`) instead of `T_{K_e}(γ_hi)`, so its width is about `0.45`. Switching that branch to `EhN/EhD` would make the bound attainable there. For `n = 3` the programs are in their positive branch, but the bound is not derived: `hBA` gets `log n ≥ 1` on the names from `e(n) ≥ 2`. Neither case matters for Robin, which concerns `n ≥ 5041`. Also open: the analytic adequacy of the eta checker. |

| [combinatorics/Combinatorics-iPRA](../docs/proofs/combinatorics/Combinatorics-iPRA/) | [Combinatorics/Averaging](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Averaging.lean), [Combinatorics/MarkovContraction](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/MarkovContraction.lean), [Combinatorics/OrbitCounting](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/OrbitCounting.lean), [Combinatorics/WallisCentral](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/WallisCentral.lean), [Combinatorics/FibonacciDiagonal](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FibonacciDiagonal.lean), [Combinatorics/SteinerResidues](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/SteinerResidues.lean), [Combinatorics/SpernerBound](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/SpernerBound.lean), [Combinatorics/TriangleDensity](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/TriangleDensity.lean), [Combinatorics/CodingBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/CodingBounds.lean), [Combinatorics/FanoPlane](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FanoPlane.lean), [Combinatorics/IsingChain](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/IsingChain.lean), [Combinatorics/DerivedDesigns](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/DerivedDesigns.lean), [Combinatorics/FirstMoment](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FirstMoment.lean), [Combinatorics/PetersenGraph](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/PetersenGraph.lean), [Combinatorics/SumFreeResidues](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/SumFreeResidues.lean) | The two averaging arguments of results 19 and 33 (the largest cut is at least the average; fewer bad pairs than colourings leave a good colouring) are derived uniformly, with the witness as a primitive recursive term. Statements of this packet derived under other packets: the two-colour graph Ramsey bounds (result on Pascal's bound) are `neighbor_split` and `pascal_bound` of [Combinatorics/RamseyBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseyBounds.lean), `R(3,3) = 6` is [Combinatorics/RamseyTriangle](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseyTriangle.lean) with [Combinatorics/RamseyPentagon](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseyPentagon.lean), and `R(3,3,3) = 17` (the first instance of result 32) is [Combinatorics/RamseySeventeen](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseySeventeen.lean) with [Combinatorics/RamseySixteen](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseySixteen.lean). Mantel's theorem (the triangle-free case of result 20) is derived by one polynomial certificate, with the Turán step, the induction on the number of parts and the multipartite count; the incidence counts of a design, the Steiner triple counts, Fisher's quadratic form and the trivial kernel of the block-sum map (results 42 to 44); the additive energy bound (result 59, third estimate); the double counts behind results 02, 34, 40, 41 and 48 (injections, packing and covering, the LYM chain count, the sum-free averaging step); one step of the Doeblin contraction (result 70); orbit–stabilizer with the orbit-counting lemma (result 63); and the monotone Wallis bounds `16^n ≤ 4n C(2n,n)²`, `4(2n+1) C(2n,n)² ≤ 3·16^n` of result 14, uniformly in `n`; and the diagonal sum `F_{n+1} = ∑_j b(n − j, j)` of result 05, uniformly in `n`. Turán's bound `r · 2e ≤ (r − 1) n²` for graphs without an `(r + 1)`-clique is derived for every `r` ([Combinatorics/Turan](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Turan.lean)), by a counting argument over all neighbourhoods instead of the packet's maximal-degree vertex. Fisher's inequality `b ≥ v` (result 43) is derived in full ([Combinatorics/FisherInequality](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FisherInequality.lean)), by the variance argument on block intersections instead of the packet's pigeonhole on a box of integer vectors, and the residues `v ≡ 1, 3 (mod 6)` of a Steiner triple system (result 44) are derived as refutations of the other residues ([Combinatorics/SteinerResidues](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/SteinerResidues.lean)). Sperner's bound and the Erdős–Ko–Rado bound are extracted from their cleared counting inequalities (results 40 and 41, [Combinatorics/SpernerBound](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/SpernerBound.lean)), and the cut balance, weak duality and the saturated-cut certificate of the max-flow theorem (result 30) are in [Combinatorics/FlowCuts](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FlowCuts.lean). The alternating face counts of the simplex, the cube and the cross-polytope (result 57) are in [Combinatorics/FaceCounts](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FaceCounts.lean). The necessity of Hall's condition and Hall's condition in regular bipartite graphs (results 28 and 29) are in [Combinatorics/HallCondition](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/HallCondition.lean), and the triangle count of a dense graph (result 20) is in [Combinatorics/TriangleDensity](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/TriangleDensity.lean). The counting steps of the Hamming, Singleton and Gilbert bounds and of perfect codes (results 48 and 49) are in [Combinatorics/CodingBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/CodingBounds.lean), and the Fano plane of result 47 as a symmetric `2-(7, 3, 1)` design is in [Combinatorics/FanoPlane](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FanoPlane.lean). The partition and two-point functions of the Ising chain and ring (result 75) are derived by the transfer matrix in [Combinatorics/IsingChain](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/IsingChain.lean). The derived and residual design counts of result 42 for `t = 2` are in [Combinatorics/DerivedDesigns](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/DerivedDesigns.lean), the counting step of the first-moment bound (result 33) and the Schröder–Hipparchus recurrence up to `H_{10}` (result 12) in [Combinatorics/FirstMoment](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FirstMoment.lean), and the Petersen graph of result 23 (cubic, diameter two, girth at least five) in [Combinatorics/PetersenGraph](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/PetersenGraph.lean). The sum-free middle third of the residues modulo `3q + 2` (result 34) is in [Combinatorics/SumFreeResidues](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/SumFreeResidues.lean). Not yet derived: the exact Turán number and its extremal graphs, the pigeonhole step of Fisher's inequality, the two covering estimates of result 59, and the constructions behind the counting steps (chains, balls, sum-free residues, group actions). Statements of the enumeration results that coincide with statements derived under earlier packets: of result 04, Pascal's rule and `b(n,k) k! (n−k)! = n!` ([Combinatorics/Binomial](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Binomial.lean)), the row sum `2^n` ([Combinatorics/BinomialIdentities](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/BinomialIdentities.lean)), Vandermonde ([Combinatorics/Vandermonde](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Vandermonde.lean)), the binomial theorem ([Combinatorics/BinomialTheorem](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/BinomialTheorem.lean)), the hockey-stick sum ([Combinatorics/HockeyStick](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/HockeyStick.lean)), symmetry and the sum of squares `b(2n,n)` ([Combinatorics/CatalanNarayana](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/CatalanNarayana.lean)); of result 05, the stars-and-bars recursion ([Combinatorics/HockeyStick](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/HockeyStick.lean)) and the Fibonacci addition law, the two sums and Cassini's identity ([Combinatorics/Fibonacci](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Fibonacci.lean)); of result 07, the Stirling recurrence ([Combinatorics/Stirling](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Stirling.lean)), `r^n = ∑ S(n,j) (r)_j` ([Combinatorics/StirlingPowers](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/StirlingPowers.lean)), the Bell recurrence ([Combinatorics/Bell](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Bell.lean)) and `B_n = ∑ S(n,j)` ([Combinatorics/BellStirling](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/BellStirling.lean)); of result 13, the Narayana sum ([Combinatorics/CatalanNarayana](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/CatalanNarayana.lean)). The remaining result groups (the twelvefold way, partitions, the Catalan families and Schröder numbers, the identification of the Wallis limit with `π` and the Catalan relative error, tableaux, matchings and flows, posets, matroids, polytopes, words, games, Ising models) have not been compared with the existing modules one by one. |

| [combinatorics/ramsey_ipra](../docs/proofs/combinatorics/ramsey_ipra/) | [Combinatorics/RamseyBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseyBounds.lean) | The counting steps as iPRA derivations about natural terms: finite thinning as an object-level finite disjunction (`thinning`), the reservoir step of `eq:stable`, the resource recurrence behind the smaller bound `eq:U`, the neighbour split and Pascal's bound for two-colour graphs, and the pivot count of `R(3,3,3) ≤ 17`. The upper bound `R(3,3) ≤ 6` is derived in [Combinatorics/RamseyTriangle](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseyTriangle.lean) for colourings given by indicator terms, by Goodman's count of monochromatic triangles, and the pentagon colouring without a monochromatic triangle (`R(3,3) > 5`) is in [Combinatorics/RamseyPentagon](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseyPentagon.lean). Finite thinning with an object-level number of classes and the primitive-recursive witness of the selected class is in [Combinatorics/UniformThinning](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/UniformThinning.lean). The Greenwood–Gleason colouring of `K_16` without a monochromatic triangle (`R(3,3,3) > 16`) is in [Combinatorics/RamseySixteen](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseySixteen.lean), and the upper bound `R(3,3,3) ≤ 17` is derived in [Combinatorics/RamseySeventeen](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/RamseySeventeen.lean) (Goodman's identity on the support of a bit vector bounds each monochromatic neighbourhood by five vertices), so `R(3,3,3) = 17`. Remaining: the mask and table codes, the bounded-search witness `W` with its check, the reservoir selection with its stage invariant, the arity induction `eq:ind` (`G(q,t,r,B(r,q,t)) = 1`), the lower-bound colourings for `R(3,3) = 6` and `R(3,3,3) = 17`, and the infinite counterexample. |
| [combinatorics/graph-ipra](../docs/proofs/combinatorics/graph-ipra/) | [Combinatorics/GraphIncidence](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/GraphIncidence.lean), [Combinatorics/GraphWalks](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/GraphWalks.lean), [Combinatorics/LaplacianBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/LaplacianBounds.lean), [Combinatorics/PlanarBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/PlanarBounds.lean), [Combinatorics/DegreeBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/DegreeBounds.lean), [Combinatorics/Turan](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Turan.lean), [Combinatorics/FlowCuts](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/FlowCuts.lean), [Combinatorics/HallCondition](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/HallCondition.lean), [Combinatorics/TriangleDensity](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/TriangleDensity.lean), [Combinatorics/PetersenGraph](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/PetersenGraph.lean), [Combinatorics/PetersenSpectrum](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/PetersenSpectrum.lean), [Combinatorics/GraphProducts](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/GraphProducts.lean), [Combinatorics/ChromaticSmall](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/ChromaticSmall.lean) | The handshake identity of Proposition 2.1 and the Laplacian energy identity of Theorem 9.1 with its nonnegativity, for a graph on `Fin k` with a symmetric matrix of integer-term weights. Also derived: the parity and regular parts of Proposition 2.1 and the edge bound and complementary degrees of Proposition 2.2; the cut identity of Proposition 2.1 and the closed-walk counts `tr(A²)` and `tr(A⁴)` of Proposition 2.4; the eigenvalue range `[0, 2Δ]` of Theorem 9.1 in cleared form; `n ≤ α χ` of Proposition 13.1 ([Combinatorics/DoubleCounting](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/DoubleCounting.lean)); Mantel's theorem with the steps of Theorem 14.1 ([Combinatorics/Mantel](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Mantel.lean)) and the bound of Theorem 14.1 for every `r` ([Combinatorics/Turan](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/Turan.lean)); and the Euler bounds `m ≤ 3n − 6`, `m ≤ 2n − 4` of Theorem 10.3 with the contradictions for `K₅` and `K_{3,3}` and the vertex of degree at most five, from Euler's relation as a hypothesis; and, of Theorems 11.1 and 12.1, the balance identity of a cut, weak duality, the saturated-cut optimality certificate and the inequality between matchings and vertex covers; the necessity of Hall's condition, the equal sides and Hall's condition of a regular bipartite graph (Theorem 12.1, Corollary 12.2; [Combinatorics/HallCondition](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/HallCondition.lean)); and the triangle count bound `E (2E − k²) ≤ k T` behind the last sentence of Theorem 14.1 for `r = 2` ([Combinatorics/TriangleDensity](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/TriangleDensity.lean)); and for the Petersen graph, the strongly regular identities with the annihilating polynomial `(t − 3)(t − 1)(t + 2)` ([Combinatorics/PetersenGraph](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/PetersenGraph.lean)) and the eigenvectors of the eigenvalues `3`, `−2` and `1` in the Kneser model ([Combinatorics/PetersenSpectrum](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/PetersenSpectrum.lean)); and the edge counts of the Cartesian, tensor, strong and lexicographic products from the degree sums ([Combinatorics/GraphProducts](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/GraphProducts.lean)); and the chromatic polynomials of trees, cycles, `K₃` and `K₄` by deletion and contraction, with Stanley's evaluation for trees and cycles ([Combinatorics/ChromaticSmall](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/ChromaticSmall.lean)). Not yet derived: the augmenting-path algorithm and the Kőnig–Hall constructions, the other results of the packet (degree sequences and Havel–Hakimi, reachability and spanning forests, cycle and cut spaces, Euler tours, Prüfer coding and the matrix–tree theorem, the Petersen graph, reconstruction, integrality and the lower eigenvalue bound, the planar constructions and colouring), the equal-degree pigeonhole of Proposition 2.2 and `tr(A³) = 6t`. |

| [diophantine-number-theory/fermat_two_squares_ipra_2026-11-22](../docs/proofs/diophantine-number-theory/fermat_two_squares_ipra_2026-11-22/) | **Fermat's theorem on sums of two squares is derived as two closed sentences of iPRA** in [DiophantineNumberTheory/FermatTwoSquares](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FermatTwoSquares.lean): `fermat_forward : [] ⊢ ∀ p. prime p → (p = 2 ∨ p mod 4 = 1) → A(p)² + B(p)² = p` with explicit witness terms `A = Aw`, `B = Bw`, and `fermat_converse : [] ⊢ ∀ a b p. prime p → a² + b² = p → p = 2 ∨ p mod 4 = 1` (`thm:main`). `thm:root-to-squares` is `root_to_squares` of [ThueGrid](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/ThueGrid.lean): for `n > 0` and `n ∣ t² + 1`, `G₁(n, t)² + G₂(n, t)² = n`, by the grid `[0, ⌊√n⌋]²`, the pigeonhole principle with search witnesses ([Core/Pigeonhole](Lean/IPRADerivations/Core/Pigeonhole.lean)) and the size argument `0 < x² + y² < 2n`. The root of `−1` modulo a prime `p ≡ 1 (mod 4)` is the witness of the bounded search for a square root of `p − 1`, justified by the first supplementary law ([QuadraticSupplements](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/QuadraticSupplements.lean), which rests on Wilson's product and Euler's criterion); the paper uses the half-factorial (`lem:root`). The remaining statements of the packet are derived in four modules. [HalfFactorialRoot](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/HalfFactorialRoot.lean): `lem:root`, the half-factorial `t = H! mod p` has `0 < t < p` and `p ∣ t² + 1` (`root_residue`), from `wilson_split`: `(p − 1 − k)! · k! ≡ (−1)^{k+1} (mod p)` for `k ≤ p − 1`, proved by induction on `k` instead of reindexing the upper half of the factorial. [TwoSquaresByDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/TwoSquaresByDescent.lean): the second construction (`sec:descent`), `two_squares_descent`, from the initial state `(t, 1, (t² + 1)/p)` and the descent step `two_descent` of [TwoSquaresDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/TwoSquaresDescent.lean), in least-multiplier form (the least `m ≥ 1` below `p` with `m p` a sum of two squares is `1`), so that the clock `eq:clock` is not needed. [TwoSquaresBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/TwoSquaresBounds.lean): a prime is not a square (`prime_not_square`), the sorted bounds `1 ≤ As(p) ≤ Bs(p) ≤ ⌊√p⌋` (`fermat_sorted`), and `thm:main` as one closed sentence with a bounded existential, `fermat_iff : [] ⊢ ∀ p. prime p → ((T(p) = 1 → p = 2 ∨ p mod 4 = 1) ∧ (p = 2 ∨ p mod 4 = 1 → T(p) = 1))`, where `T(p)` is the bit of the search `∃ c < (p + 1)². (c mod (p + 1))² + ⌊c/(p + 1)⌋² = p`. |

| [diophantine-number-theory/lagrange_four_squares_ipra_2026-11-22](../docs/proofs/diophantine-number-theory/lagrange_four_squares_ipra_2026-11-22/) | **Lagrange's four-square theorem is derived as a closed sentence of iPRA with explicit witness terms**: `lagrange : [] ⊢ ∀ n. W₀(n)² + W₁(n)² + W₂(n)² + W₃(n)² = n` in [DiophantineNumberTheory/FourSquares](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FourSquares.lean) (`thm:main`), with `witness_le : W_i(n) ≤ n`. The witnesses are the base-`(n + 1)` digits of the least code below `(n + 1)⁴` whose digits have squares summing to `n`; the theorem is that this bounded search succeeds. (1) Euler's identity and the algebra of one descent step, with signed forms entering only through squares of absolute differences ([FourSquaresAlgebra](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FourSquaresAlgebra.lean)). (2) For an odd prime `p = 2h + 1`: `p ∣ a² + b² + 1` with `a, b ≤ h`, by the pigeonhole principle on two lists of residues ([FourSquaresSeed](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FourSquaresSeed.lean)). (3) One step of the descent, `N(x) = m p`, `2 ≤ m < p` to `N(w) = m' p`, `1 ≤ m' < m`, with the new tuple written without division ([FourSquaresDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FourSquaresDescent.lean)). (4) Primes by the least multiplier (a bounded search and `ex_least` replace the iteration of the descent with fuel), products by Euler's identity, and all `n` by strong induction through the least prime factor ([FourSquares](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FourSquares.lean)). Deviations from the paper: the residue step is used for every multiplier, the even tie being excluded directly (the paper halves the tuple for even `m`); the witness functions are a bounded search rather than the factor loop of `sections/04_assembly.tex`, so the "Factor-loop invariant" and the coding of `sections/07_coding.tex` are not derived as stated. |
