| [diophantine-number-theory/mordell_weil_ipra](../docs/proofs/diophantine-number-theory/mordell_weil_ipra/) | [Core/Bezout](Lean/IPRADerivations/Core/Bezout.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/SquareSplit](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/SquareSplit.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/SquareArea](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/SquareArea.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/EllipticE1](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/EllipticE1.lean), [Foundations/BoundedReflection](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/BoundedReflection.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/HeightContraction](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/HeightContraction.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/EllipticFormulas](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/EllipticFormulas.lean); the divisibility step of `lem:ratroots` (a reduced root `m/n` has `m` dividing the constant and `n` the leading coefficient, in every degree) is `constant_dvd` and `leading_dvd` of [Foundations/RationalRootConstant](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/RationalRootConstant.lean) and [Foundations/RationalRootDegree](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/RationalRootDegree.lean) `thm:products` is partly derived for `u = 1` (`e1_no_point` of [DiophantineNumberTheory/EllipticE1](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/EllipticE1.lean)). |
| [diophantine-number-theory/number_theory_ipra_2026-11-22](../docs/proofs/diophantine-number-theory/number_theory_ipra_2026-11-22/) | A survey packet of 106 theorem environments, reconciled statement by statement in the [number theory claim audit](NUMBER-THEORY-CLAIMS.md): 73 derived, 25 derived in part, 8 open. Most derivations are the modules of the dedicated packets. Written for this packet: [PrimesModFour](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PrimesModFour.lean) (`an:progressions`), [ThreeSquares](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/ThreeSquares.lean) and [ThreeSquaresPowers](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/ThreeSquaresPowers.lean) (`qr:three-obstruction`), [ResidueCharacter](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/ResidueCharacter.lean) (`qr:character`, `qr:rational-four`), [TwoSquaresObstruction](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/TwoSquaresObstruction.lean) and [TwoSquaresValuation](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/TwoSquaresValuation.lean) (`qr:two-obstruction`, both assertions; the necessity half of `qr:two-classification`), [WilsonConverse](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/WilsonConverse.lean) (`el:wilson`, every prime and the converse), [OrderDivides](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/OrderDivides.lean) (`el:order`), [DigitRules](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/DigitRules.lean) and [DigitAlternating](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/DigitAlternating.lean) (`el:digits`: the rules modulo `b − 1` and `b + 1` and the last digits), [PerfectNumbers](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/PerfectNumbers.lean) (`el:perfect`, the direction of Euclid), [ChebyshevPi](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ChebyshevPi.lean), [ChebyshevUpper](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ChebyshevUpper.lean) and [ChebyshevCorollaries](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ChebyshevCorollaries.lean) (`an:binomial`, second and third inequalities; `an:chebyshev`, both bounds), [MobiusInversionReverse](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusInversionReverse.lean) (`el:convolution`: unit and distributivity; `el:mobius`: the reverse direction), [MertensTotient](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MertensTotient.lean) (`el:summatory`, first identity), [LinearDiophantine](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/LinearDiophantine.lean) and [LinearCongruence](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/LinearCongruence.lean) (`el:linear`, `el:linearcong`: solvability, with explicit solutions), [SieveCriterion](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveCriterion.lean) (`el:sieve`: the least-factor criterion), [PellFamily](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellFamily.lean) (`dio:pell-family`), [OmegaMean](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/OmegaMean.lean) (`an:means`, first identity), [FareyMediant](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FareyMediant.lean) (`dio:farey`), [PythagoreanScaling](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PythagoreanScaling.lean) (`dio:all-triples`), [SquareResidues](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/SquareResidues.lean) (`qr:squares-half`), [TwoSquaresDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/TwoSquaresDescent.lean) (`qr:two-descent`), [OrderOfPower](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/OrderOfPower.lean) (`qr:primitive-count`: the order of a power, for every modulus), [FrobeniusSum](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/FrobeniusSum.lean) (`el:binomial`: `(x + y)^p ≡ x^p + y^p`), [KummerDigits](Lean/IPRADerivations/Combinatorics/KummerDigits.lean) (`el:kummer`: the digit-sum identity), [ValuationAdd](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/ValuationAdd.lean) (`ag:valuation`: the ultrametric inequality with its equality case, and the valuation of a gcd), [QuadraticRingFive](Lean/IPRADerivations/Algebra/QuadraticRingFive.lean) and [QuadraticRingFiveIrreducible](Lean/IPRADerivations/Algebra/QuadraticRingFiveIrreducible.lean) (`ag:norm`, `ag:nonufd`: the norm `a² + 5b²` takes neither the value `2` nor `3`, so `2`, `3`, `1 ± θ` are irreducible, no two are associates, and `2` is not prime), [LegendreSieve](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LegendreSieve.lean) (`an:sieve`: the inclusion-exclusion identity for every modulus), [SieveSurvivors](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SieveSurvivors.lean) (`an:sievepi`: `π(x) = S(x, ⌊√x⌋) + π(⌊√x⌋) − 1`), [TauParity](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/TauParity.lean) (`el:squareparity`: `τ(n)` is odd exactly when `n` is a square), [ContinuedFractionTail](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/ContinuedFractionTail.lean) (`dio:cf-error`: the error identities and bounds for a tail `t = u/v`), [LiouvilleName](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/LiouvilleName.lean) (`tr:name`: the truncations `P_n/q_n` of the Liouville number, `r_n < 1/9`, the tail bound `r_m − r_n ≤ 2 · 10^{−(n+1)!}`, the modulus `2^{−n}`, the gap `< q_n^{−h}` and lowest terms), [FiniteIndependence](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/FiniteIndependence.lean) (`an:independence`: `#(A ∩ B) · N = #A · #B` for divisibility by `a` and coprimality to `b`, `gcd(a, b) = 1`, in a sample of length `a b c`), [LiouvilleLambda](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/LiouvilleLambda.lean) (`el:liouville`: `Ω(a b) = Ω(a) + Ω(b)` and the multiplicativity of `λ`). [PellCollision](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellCollision.lean), [PellSequence](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellSequence.lean) and [PellExistence](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellExistence.lean) (`dio:pell-existence`: bounded Pell existence for every nonsquare `D ≥ 2`), [CfReal](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/CfReal.lean) (`dio:cf-real`: `|p_M/q_M − p_N/q_N| ≤ q_N^{−2}` and the rational name `r_k = p_{2k+2}/q_{2k+2}` with modulus `2^{−2k−2}`, for every primitive recursive coefficient function; the growth bounds of `dio:continuants`), [CfValue](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/CfValue.lean) (`dio:continuants`: the nested fraction `[a_0; …, a_m]` is exactly `(p_m, q_m)`), [CfTail](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/CfTail.lean) (`dio:cf-error`: `[a_0; …, a_m, u/v] = (u p_m + v p_{m−1}, u q_m + v q_{m−1})`), [GcdLcm](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/GcdLcm.lean) (`el:valuation`: `gcd · lcm = ab`), [OrderProduct](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/OrderProduct.lean) (`qr:orders`: the order of a product with coprime orders), [MobiusMult](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/MobiusMult.lean) (`el:mobiuscancel`: `μ` is multiplicative), [SigmaR](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/SigmaR.lean) (`el:divisorfunc`: `σ_r` and `τ = σ_0` are multiplicative, `τ(p^a) = a + 1`, `σ_r(p^a) = ∑_{j ≤ a} (p^r)^j`). Open: the polynomial root bound and the existence of primitive roots, the Euler product error and the name of `ζ(s)`, the three partition theorems, and the transcendence of the Liouville number. Derived in part, among others: the description of all solutions of a linear equation and the count of the solutions of a linear congruence, the marking loop of the sieve of Eratosthenes, the divisor sum of Liouville's function, the count of primitive roots, sufficiency in the two-square classification, the uniqueness of the canonical continued fraction, the error term of the inclusion-exclusion sieve, the squarefree decomposition and the parity of `σ`. |
| [diophantine-number-theory/pell_ipra_2026-11-15](../docs/proofs/diophantine-number-theory/pell_ipra_2026-11-15/) | [DiophantineNumberTheory/Pell](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/Pell.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/Recurrences](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/Recurrences.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/IrrationalSqrt](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/IrrationalSqrt.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellDescent.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellNegDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellNegDescent.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellNegUnits](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellNegUnits.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellGenDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellGenDescent.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellAll](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellAll.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellGcd](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellGcd.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellModular](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellModular.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellOneSided](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellOneSided.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellCFStep](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellCFStep.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellCF](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellCF.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellGeneral](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellGeneral.lean), [DiophantineNumberTheory/PellLegendre](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PellLegendre.lean) |
| [diophantine-number-theory/quadratic_reciprocity_ipra](../docs/proofs/diophantine-number-theory/quadratic_reciprocity_ipra/) | **The law of quadratic reciprocity is derived as a closed sentence of iPRA**: `quadratic_reciprocity : [] ⊢ sentence` in [DiophantineNumberTheory/QuadraticReciprocity](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/QuadraticReciprocity.lean), where `sentence` ([QuadraticReciprocityGoal](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/QuadraticReciprocityGoal.lean)) says: for all `p`, `q`, if both are prime and odd and `p ≠ q`, then `par (signBit p q + signBit q p) = par (⌊p/2⌋ ⌊q/2⌋)`, with `signBit p a` the bit of the bounded formula "no `x < p` has `x² ≡ a (mod p)`". The route follows the packet. (1) The lattice count `floorSum p q + floorSum q p = ⌊p/2⌋⌊q/2⌋` for odd coprime `p`, `q` ([RectangleCount](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/RectangleCount.lean)). (2) The folded residues `b_i = min(a i mod p, p − a i mod p)` form a permutation of `[1, h]` ([FoldedResidues](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FoldedResidues.lean), [FoldedPermutation](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/FoldedPermutation.lean)), which gives the parity conversion `par (floorSum p a) = par N_p(a)` ([EisensteinParity](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/EisensteinParity.lean)) and Gauss's product `(p − 1)^{N_p(a)} a^h ≡ 1 (mod p)` ([GaussProduct](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/GaussProduct.lean)); `−1` is the residue `p − 1` throughout. (3) Euler's criterion from the involution `x ↦ a x⁻¹` ([UnitInvolution](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/UnitInvolution.lean)) and the pairing lemma [Foundations/InvolutionPairing](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/InvolutionPairing.lean): `(p − 1)! ≡ a^h` for a nonresidue ([PairProduct](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/PairProduct.lean)), Wilson's product `(p − 1)! ≡ p − 1` ([WilsonProduct](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/WilsonProduct.lean)), and `a^h ≡ 1` for a residue ([ResidueFacts](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/ResidueFacts.lean)). (4) Gauss's lemma `par N_p(a) = 1 − [a is a square]` ([GaussLemma](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/GaussLemma.lean)), the identification of the search bit with `signBit`, Eisenstein's lemma `signBit p a = par (floorSum p a)` and the assembly ([QuadraticReciprocity](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/QuadraticReciprocity.lean)). (5) The supplementary laws and the witnesses: `signBit p (p − 1) = par ⌊p/2⌋` (`minus_one_law`), the root `x = wit < p` with `x² ≡ a` when `signBit p a = 0` and the exclusion of every root when `signBit p a = 1` (`root_of_sign`, `no_root_of_sign`), the short witness `a^{t+1} mod p` for `p = 4t + 3` (`short_root`), all in [QuadraticSupplements](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/QuadraticSupplements.lean), and `signBit p 2 = par ⌊h (h + 1)/2⌋` with `h (h + 1)/2 = (p² − 1)/8` (`two_law` of [SecondSupplement](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/SecondSupplement.lean)). The table of signs by `p mod 8` is `table_one`, `table_three`, `table_five`, `table_seven` of [SupplementTable](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/SupplementTable.lean) (the bits of `(−1/p)` and `(2/p)` for `p = 8t + 1, 3, 5, 7`), and the residue-class formulation of the law is `reciprocity_one_left`, `reciprocity_one_right` (`signBit p q = signBit q p` when `p ≡ 1` or `q ≡ 1 (mod 4)`) and `reciprocity_three` (`signBit p q + signBit q p = 1` when `p ≡ q ≡ 3 (mod 4)`) of [ReciprocityClasses](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/ReciprocityClasses.lean). |

The complete finite-net infimum result and uniqueness in the name metric are checked in [Analysis/RationalCubeInfimumComparison](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalCubeInfimumComparison.lean). The source point-name uniqueness proof for regular cube-presented competitors is checked in [Analysis/RationalCubeMinimizerUniqueness](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalCubeMinimizerUniqueness.lean). The full factorization schema remains open. The regularity of the sum name of `prop:name-arithmetic` on a common dyadic scale is `add_regular` of [Foundations/DyadicNames](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/DyadicNames.lean). The uniqueness modulus of `cor:strong-convexity` (midpoint drop and two approximate minima give the squared-distance bound, then the distance bound from an explicit schedule) is `rational_squared_bound` and `distance_budget` of [Foundations/StrongConvexity](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/StrongConvexity.lean), derived under the finite-use packet. The original rational annihilator is checked in [Analysis/RationalOriginalAnnihilator](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalOriginalAnnihilator.lean). The actual denominator clearing and cleared-entry height are checked in [Analysis/RationalMatrixClearingBounds](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalMatrixClearingBounds.lean). The maximum minor and least free column are checked in [Analysis/RationalMaximalMinors](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalMaximalMinors.lean) and [Analysis/RationalMinorFreeColumn](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalMinorFreeColumn.lean). The exact-cutoff corollary is checked in [Analysis/RationalPhiCoverPrefix](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalPhiCoverPrefix.lean). The explicitly listed exception bound is checked in [Analysis/RationalPhiExceptionalCover](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/RationalPhiExceptionalCover.lean). The nonrise index of the rational window is `floor_nonrise` of [Foundations/RationalWindow](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/RationalWindow.lean). The estimate at the selected endpoints of the represented window is `selected_increase` of [Foundations/RealWindow](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/RealWindow.lean). The candidate starts, the choice of `K` and the final margins of the two window theorems, the analytic producers and the certificate theorems `dio:height` and `dio:completion` of the Diophantine section are not derived; `dio:family` (the integer points of `y^m = x^{mr} + x + 1`, uniformly in `m ≥ 3`, `r ≥ 1`) is [DiophantineNumberTheory/SuperellipticFamily](Lean/IPRADerivations/DiophantineNumberTheory/SuperellipticFamily.lean), without `dio:height`, by the power gap directly. A reusable induction principle with a shifting parameter is [Foundations/ShiftedInduction](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/ShiftedInduction.lean). Statements derived under other modules (each checked against the statement, 19 November 2026): `logic:noeval` is `no_universal_primitive_recursive_evaluator` of [Foundations/UniversalEvaluatorObstruction](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/UniversalEvaluatorObstruction.lean) and `no_universal_evaluator` of [Foundations/EmbeddingBasics](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/EmbeddingBasics.lean). | [foundations/ipra_framework_2026-11-15](../docs/proofs/foundations/ipra_framework_2026-11-15/) | [Foundations/PrecisionArithmetic](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/PrecisionArithmetic.lean), [Foundations/LeastBoundedWitness](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/LeastBoundedWitness.lean); the cleared numerator inequality cores for finite-tail replacement and effective inversion under apartness, finite-certificate composition and the specification of bounded existential search are checked. Represented tail equality, the actual inverse-name precision schedule and its representation adapter remain; finite descent with explicit range is the theorem `terminal` of [Foundations/BoundedDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/BoundedDescent.lean) with the terminal test "height zero", under that module's extra clause that the step fixes terminal states (without it the iterate after the height first reaches zero is not controlled); the search from a supplied height bound is `witness_of_bound` and `decide_of_bound` of [Foundations/LeastBoundedWitness](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/LeastBoundedWitness.lean) (with the complete list in Foundations/BoundedEnumeration); for the algebra of dyadic Cauchy names, the synchronized sum estimate is `sum_modulus` of Foundations/PrecisionArithmetic and the monotone normalization of a modulus is [Foundations/ModulusNormalization](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/ModulusNormalization.lean); the assembly of the sum name with its representation predicate is not derived. The no-uniform-zero-decision theorem is a statement about computable procedures, not an iPRA derivation. "Finite descent with explicit range" is false as stated (the height can reach 0 before step `h(x)`, after which the step is unconstrained); the repaired lemma, with the guarded step `T'(x) = x` at height 0, is `explicit_range` of [Foundations/GuardedDescent](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/GuardedDescent.lean), and `explicit_range_fix` gives the unguarded iterate when `T` fixes valid height-0 states. In "Safe alternatives", the core of clause (ii) (the sample lower bound and the reciprocal error) is `apart_lower`/`inverse_error` of [Foundations/PrecisionArithmetic](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/PrecisionArithmetic.lean). "No uniform zero-decision from Cauchy names" is a statement about computable procedures, not an iPRA derivation. |

Statements derived under other modules (each checked against the statement, 19 November 2026): `thm:finite-erasure` is `exact_erasure`, `metric_erasure`, `quotient_erasure` of [Foundations/ObserverErasure](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/ObserverErasure.lean). Partly derived: truth-certificate soundness of `thm:strategy-reflection` (`strategy_sound`, `bounded_strategy` of [Foundations/StrategyRealization](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/StrategyRealization.lean)) and the invariant induction of `thm:path-extension` (`trace_invariant_all` of [Foundations/TraceInvariant](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/TraceInvariant.lean)); `cor:raw-demand-adequacy` (one coordinate) is `raw_adequate` and `raw_least` of [Foundations/RawDemand](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/RawDemand.lean); `thm:contract-native` is the composition of `contract_iff_sequent` of [Foundations/OpenContracts](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/OpenContracts.lean) with `pra_iff_native` of [ConciseIPRA/NativeBiInterpretation](../ConciseIPRA/Lean/ConciseIPRA/NativeBiInterpretation.lean), both checked (the composite is not packaged as one Lean statement). `thm:coherence-trichotomy` is partly derived: the positive implications through `exact_erasure`, `metric_erasure` of [Foundations/ObserverErasure](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/ObserverErasure.lean), `trace_invariant_all` of [Foundations/TraceInvariant](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/TraceInvariant.lean) and `regular_of_budget` of [Foundations/TelescopingNames](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/TelescopingNames.lean); the separations are counterexamples at the metalevel. |

[Foundations/SplitBranches](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/SplitBranches.lean) derives the scalar nonzero-factor positivity/size kernels of `lem:split-factor` and the natural leading-term comparison from a supplied isolated-leading-term inequality. The actual polynomial-evaluation/degree adapter and finite zero-factor selector remain separate. Quantitative uniqueness (`thm:ode-unique`) at the nodes is [Foundations/OdeUniqueness](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/OdeUniqueness.lean): the one-step comparison of two controlled candidates through their Euler predictions (`unique_step`, signed displacements as pairs of natural tables, also the perturbation recurrence `eq:ode-perturbation`) and `eq:ode-uniquefinite` (`uniqueness_const`); the accuracy schedule and the equality of the name families remain. The interpretation through numerical presentations (`def:presented-sort`, `prop:setoid-interpretation`) is `sound` of [Foundations/SetoidInterpretation](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/SetoidInterpretation.lean), for one sort with equational atoms and any target theory (many sorts and relation symbols are not treated). Statements derived under other modules (each checked against the statement, 19 November 2026): "Finite monotone demand closure" is `capped_closure`, `capped_closure_least`, `iter_le_prefixed` of [Foundations/FiniteStabilization](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/FiniteStabilization.lean) and `demand_closure` of [Foundations/DemandClosure](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/DemandClosure.lean) (packed vectors from the zero seed); "Quotient observation principle" is `quotient_erasure`/`sealed_consumer` of [Foundations/ObserverErasure](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/ObserverErasure.lean) (bounded certificates); "STRIVE bounded-formula compilation" (6.1)–(6.7) is [Foundations/StriveCompilation](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/StriveCompilation.lean), with the canonical one-point certificates. |

| [geometry/helly-ipra](../docs/proofs/geometry/helly-ipra/) | [Geometry/HellyCertificates](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellyCertificates.lean), [Geometry/HellyPlane](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellyPlane.lean), [Geometry/HellySpace](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellySpace.lean) | The certificate identities of `thm:main` and `lem:radon` as iPRA derivations about integer terms: the elimination step of Radon's lemma (the new columns annihilate the pivot row and the earlier rows, the dual rows stay dual), the equal masses and moments of the two parts of the affine dependence, the mass and barycentre of a glued row, the mass, barycentre and sign of a compressed row, the projected row and the lift of the coordinate elimination of `thm:poly`, and the counting step of the centerpoint corollary `cor:cent`. The case `d = 1`, `n = 3` of `thm:main` is derived completely in [Geometry/HellyLine](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellyLine.lean), with the median of the three supplied points as the witness term, and the case `d = 1` for an object-level number of intervals is [Geometry/HellyIntervals](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellyIntervals.lean), with the least right endpoint as the witness term. For four points in the plane the Radon partition is explicit in [Geometry/RadonPartition](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/RadonPartition.lean) (the dependence by signed areas, its positive and negative parts as terms, their equal masses and moments and their disjoint supports); the normalization by the positive mass and the collinear case are not assembled. The membership statement of `thm:main` is derived in [Geometry/HellyPlane](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellyPlane.lean) for a dependence in any dimension (every half-space that contains the points `p_j`, `j ≠ i`, contains the Radon point in homogeneous coordinates, the row being chosen by the disjunction `Z_i⁺ = 0 ∨ Z_i⁻ = 0`), with the full statement for four sets in the plane, and in [Geometry/HellySpace](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellySpace.lean) for five sets in space with the dependence by signed volumes. Still to derive for `d ≥ 2`: the elimination loop with its invariant `A_{<t} V = 0`, `L V = I_m` over all rows, the induction over the levels `k = h, …, n` of the gluing with the mask scan, the iteration of the compression to at most `d + 1` positive entries, the full elimination and reconstruction of `thm:poly`, the assembly of the precision-preserving theorem `thm:approx` (its weighted error bound is `weighted_precision` of [Geometry/HellyPrecision](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/HellyPrecision.lean)) and the four-triangle obstruction. |

| Analytic geometry affine conic pullback | [Geometry/Distinct/AnalyticConicAffine](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/Distinct/AnalyticConicAffine.lean), [Geometry/Distinct/AnalyticConicRationalAffine](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/Distinct/AnalyticConicRationalAffine.lean) | The actual primitive-recursive coefficient record gives evaluation covariance, the discriminant multiplier, and zero-locus equivalence over integer coefficients and coordinates, including singular maps. The rational pullback retains evaluation and discriminant covariance as equalities of the actual normalized codes with positive denominators. The actual inverse map now returns both normalized coordinate roundtrips and transports evaluation and the zero locus under a nonzero determinant. Analytic classification remains open. |
| Analytic geometry inverse affine map | [Geometry/Distinct/AnalyticConicRationalInverse](Lean/IPRADerivations/Geometry/Distinct/AnalyticConicRationalInverse.lean) | The exact affine inverse code uses the positive squared determinant as its denominator. Both compositions recover the original normalized coordinates. The actual conic evaluator and its zero locus therefore transport along the checked inverse program. |

step `a² P(A_k) ≤ E[S_n² 1_{A_k}]` of `thm:kolmogorov` on a two-block product law), [Probability/KolmogorovAssembly](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/KolmogorovAssembly.lean) (the sum over the disjoint first-crossing events, `a² P(max_k S_k² ≥ a²) ≤ E[S_n²]`; with the past/future split [Probability/ProductSplit](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/ProductSplit.lean), the first-crossing indicators [Probability/FirstCrossing](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/FirstCrossing.lean) and the step data [Probability/KolmogorovIid](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/KolmogorovIid.lean), [Probability/KolmogorovIidGlue](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/KolmogorovIidGlue.lean) derives `thm:kolmogorov` for an iid sample, `kolmogorov_iid`: `a² P(max_{k≤n} W² (S_k − k μ)² ≥ a²) ≤ E[W² (S_n − n μ)²]` (cleared, the threshold a body `a(M, x)`), and with the second moment `E[W² (S_n − n μ)²] = n W² σ²` of [Probability/KolmogorovVariance](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/KolmogorovVariance.lean) in its final form `a² P(max_{k≤n} W² (S_k − k μ)² ≥ a²) ≤ n W² σ²` (`kolmogorov_variance`); the marginal law of the first coordinates, used to place a block statement inside a longer sample, is [Probability/ProductMarginal](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/ProductMarginal.lean); the block step of `thm:dyadic-slln` (Kolmogorov for the first `N` coordinates inside a sample of length `N + m`) is [Probability/KolmogorovBlock](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/KolmogorovBlock.lean), and generic versions of the union and geometric steps also exist on their own: the union over events is `union_bound` of [Probability/UnionUtility](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/UnionUtility.lean) and the finite geometric sum of the block bounds (`2^r b_r ≤ C` gives `∑_{r<R} b_r ≤ 2C`) is `geometric_cauchy` of [Foundations/OdeRefinement](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/OdeRefinement.lean) with `D` the partial sums; the instantiation of `block_kolmogorov` at the thresholds `2e 2^r W`, uniformly in the block `r`, is derived ([Probability/DyadicSLLN](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/DyadicSLLN.lean) `block_hyp`: `2e²W²2^r P_r ≤ (WQ ∸ S²)T_H`, via [Probability/DyadicBlock](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/DyadicBlock.lean) `block_cross`), as is the finite geometric union bound ([Probability/DyadicUnion](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/DyadicUnion.lean) `dyadic_union`); the covering of the bad event by the blocks ([Probability/DyadicCover](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/DyadicCover.lean) `cover_pt`), the union over the blocks ([Probability/DyadicAssembly](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/DyadicAssembly.lean) (2enW)² ≤ |WS_n − nS|²) ≤ (WQ ∸ S²) T_H`, i.e. `P(∃ n ∈ [2^K, H] : |X̄_n − μ| ≥ 2ε) ≤ σ²/(ε²2^K)` for every finite horizon `H`, with constant `1` in place of the packet's `4`; for an iid sample the [Analysis/BaselFinite](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/BaselFinite.lean); the finite stages of `cor:countable-tail` and `thm:layer-cake` (truncated outcome index and value) are the tail-sum formula of [Probability/FiniteHolder](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/FiniteHolder.lean) for every rational conjugate pair `p = k/i`, `q = k/(k − i)` (natural values `U = X^i`, `V = Y^{k−i}`, certified bounds `A^p ≥ E[U^p]`, `B^q ≥ E[V^q]` give `E[UV] ≤ AB`; the case `p = q = 2` is also the exact `cauchy_schwarz` of [Probability/Expectation](Lean/IPRADerivations/Probability/Expectation.lean)), and statement are not formalized. `thm:vitali` (finite core: the truncation with separate tails for `X_n` and `X`) and `cor:bounded-convergence` are `vitali` and `bounded_convergence` of (`quantum_uncertainty`). The finite grid form of `thm:stieltjes` (a grid CDF `F` and a piecewise-linear `g`, with the increments `a(n) = F(n) − F(n−1)`) is the summation by parts `abel_parts` of [AnalyticNumberTheory/AbelParts](Lean/IPRADerivations/AnalyticNumberTheory/AbelParts.lean), `Σ_{n≤N} g(n) a(n) = F(N) g(N+1) − Σ_{n≤N} F(n)(g(n+1) − g(n))` with signed increments split in ℕ; the passage to improper limits is metalevel. ## Verification

At the source based on `3c9d1097b3`, including the complete finite nonmonic
shrinking-step theorem, the root build completed 2,351 jobs and emitted
9,265 axiom reports for 8,756 distinct declarations. Every report uses only
`propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. All nine import closure gates
passed: 3,770 modules in the portable arithmetic closure and 5,252 in the
independent `Diophantine` closure.

The six new modules contain 1,151 lines, 112 public theorems and 144 public
declarations, each with a fully qualified axiom probe. Independent full
source/math reviews checked the original coefficient apartness, derived
nonconstancy, actual selected coefficient representations, native and
normalized balanced-radius comparisons, and all program fields. They
checked that only genuine decoded denominator squares are cancelled;
selected-norm positivity is not an extra input, and zero residuals and
radius numerators are covered.
Actual center-to-starting radius transport and both displacement branches
were checked. Two independent critical reviews accepted the complete
source step-bound assembly and its 15-variable closed input/output maps.
The power calculation includes zero decay numerators and zero iteration
counter; it cancels only proved positive denominator powers.

`GaussianNonmonicStepBound.source_step_bound_closed` now proves the finite
source travel estimate r0<=U*C0*rho^j, with C0=B0*N*2^N/lambda, for the actual
starting balanced radius. `source_displacement_bound_closed` and
`source_displacement_program_bound_closed` prove the same bound for the
unchanged constructed displacement and its actual PR3 query. The input
has original polynomial shape, arbitrary positive-degree coefficient
apartness lambda=L/M>0, U=A/B>=1, the original point disk, Q<=rho^N,
C0>=1 and the actual residual bound B0*Q^j. Nonconstancy, maximum selection,
balanced-radius comparisons and the final native power budget are all
derived internally. No monicity, nonzero-leading-coefficient or canonical
original-input restriction is added. The source disk U-1 is relaxed to U,
and the finite estimate supports rho in [0,1]. Zero residuals are included.

`GaussianNonmonicTravelCoefficientBound` supplies the actual native
coefficient/radius inequality and derives nonconstancy from the original
apartness guard. `GaussianNonmonicTravelComparison` binds its literal
normalized rational operands and actual returned index/radius/values.
`GaussianTravelResidualClearing` specializes the true residual budget,
while `RationalShrinkingStepPowerBound` raises and compares the internal
powers and reflects their order. `GaussianNonmonicDisplacementRadius`
connects the selected center radius to the actual starting radius, with
no polynomial-shape or RD premise. The preceding uniform nonmonic RD,
finite approximate-root and concrete monic root-support/adequacy results
remain checked at their unchanged actual programs.

The general coefficient-name construction now retains every supplied
coefficient, including an undecided or zero leading coefficient. The original
h+2 apartness test derives the whole-tail lambda lower bound. All-coefficient
Horner perturbation includes the changing leading sample. Actual source
constants and finite Bernoulli/block estimates discharge all rate and clock
budgets. The actual finite product is uniformly bounded by the computed R
using a complementary-gap QF induction; no global product law is assumed.
The changing-precision PR4 trajectory uses P_j sampled at j+L, with derived
updates, canonicality, sampled nonconstancy and initial B residual. The computed
sampled-step theorem discharges every finite travel obligation except the
legitimate current point-disk and residual hypotheses.
The adjacent-sample drift theorem derives B Q^j/(8D) throughout the
computed R disk from coefficient coherence and the actual precision budget,
including the changing leading coefficient.

The actual residual recurrence and position-margin update now establish a
source-only simultaneous invariant by genuine QF induction. The next disk is
proved before the residual step uses coefficient drift. Actual finite gd
telescoping and the computed J/M schedules prove the two original general-FTA
main inequalities, including every later original coefficient sample and the
unchanged upper degree N. The actual returned root program is pointCN and lies
in R. Actual same-precision V_t uses all original coefficient observations and
alpha_t; its derived (2S+1/4)2^-t bound and bl(4S+1) shift construct a canonical
fast polynomial-value zero name. No global invariant, root-name validity,
polynomial-value coherence or zero-name law is a source premise.

The [general polynomial observer](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianGeneralPolynomialEvaluation.lean)
now works at any bounded fast Gaussian point name, with separate numerical
coefficient and point data. All N+1 coefficients contribute to its derived
modulus (N+1)U^N plus the actual weighted Horner Lipschitz sum. Degree zero
and zero leading coefficients are included. The
[general value-name program](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianGeneralPolynomialValueName.lean)
is canonical and fast from those source contracts alone. An arbitrary raw
K*2^-s envelope implies its zero predicate by finite fixed-query slack removal;
no comparison between K and the normalization modulus is assumed.
[The actual general FTA adapter](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianGeneralFTAValueZero.lean)
derives that envelope at the returned root and proves zero of this same
generic polynomial-value program. Its original raw observations are identified;
the independently shifted normalized programs are not equated.

The [constant coefficient-name compiler](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianConstantCoefficientNames.lean)
uses one fixed PR3 program and numerical data (N,C). It reads the original
descending entry C[N-i], proves coefficient coherence without a premise,
and computes dyadic apartness from the actual positive decoded denominator
and a nonzero native coefficient norm. Its closed source-input theorem needs
only 1<=k<=N and that nonzero coefficient. The
[original-polynomial frontend](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianConstantPolynomialEvaluation.lean)
now proves entry equality and a genuine guarded QF Horner-prefix equality;
list length gives exact evaluation of the original numerical polynomial.
Its fixed root/value programs have proved pointCN and zero predicates, and
the original raw polynomial evaluations at the actual computed root precision
are bounded by 2^-t. Only length and one nonzero positive-degree coefficient
are supplied. Equality of arbitrary outer list codes is not asserted.

The [actual Euclidean name relation](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianNameEquivalence.lean)
now has diagonal/cross-precision equivalence, equivalence laws on proper
names and actual program-zero transport. Every auxiliary precision is
removed by the proved finite integer-gap query. The
[independent normalizer theorem](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianValueNormalizerExtensionality.lean)
keeps both numerical moduli, persistent data and actual precision shifts;
a vanishing raw discrepancy gives cross-equivalent normalized programs,
including zero moduli and without a modulus-order assumption.
[General polynomial extensionality](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianGeneralPolynomialExtensionality.lean)
derives that discrepancy from equivalent original coefficient and point
names on a computed common proof disk. All N+1 coefficients participate.
The output programs retain their own original disks, data and shifts, and
their zero predicates agree. Degree zero and zero leading samples are
included. The [actual degree-padding program](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianPolynomialDegreePadding.lean)
retains original coefficients through N and returns literal zero above it.
Two guarded QF Horner-prefix inductions prove exact padded evaluation for
N<=P, including degree zero. The
[different-degree comparison](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianPolynomialDegreeExtensionality.lean)
compares two independently normalized original value programs through their
actual extensions at N+M. Their original disks, data and shifts are retained;
equivalent padded coefficient names and point names give equivalent values
and zero predicates. No outer list-code equality is used. Completed-field
ring/evaluation realization remains separate.

The [natural scalar action](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianRationalNaturalScale.lean)
and [actual finite Gaussian sum](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianRationalFiniteSum.lean)
now have canonical-output, zero/unit, recurrence, shifted-first-term,
bounded-entry congruence, addition and multiplication transport proofs.
The sum laws use genuine QF prefix inductions over the actual generated
lists; raw entries retain their canonical recoding where needed. These
provide the finite algebra for the now-proved
[inverse-binomial row](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianInverseBinomialRows.lean).
Actual finite-sum Pascal recurrences and compiled bounded backward induction
derive every row. The [literal source identity](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianRationalInverseTranslation.lean)
then binds ordinary binomial coefficients and the actual translated list:
the canonical recode of original coefficient a_k is
sum_{r=0}^{N-k} binom(k+r,k) b_{k+r} (-z)^r.
Only original list length and k<=N are assumed; N=0 and raw noncanonical
entries are supported. The actual PR4 output returns both equal quantities
and the translated list. Source reconciliation and the remaining packet
claims are not inferred from this identity.

The [coded finite-chain producer](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/CodedViableChain.lean)
stores the actual h+1 candidates and derives its object-language admissibility
and an independent PR bound on the code. A genuine QF induction checks any
numerical admissible code in the converse direction, yielding bounded coded
existence iff viability at the supplied start. The
[automatic initial selector](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/ViableChainSelection.lean)
then finds the least viable start and returns a success-tagged chain or the
literal failure output. Its success bit is equivalent to bounded existence
of any admissible full-horizon code; the entire tagged output has a proved
bound depending only on candidate bounds and horizon. No host admissibility,
successful search or output-law premise is supplied.
[Arbitrary-level coded suffixes](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/CodedViableSuffix.lean)
now prove the source equivalence for every numerical i<=h and a<B(i):
V(h-i,a)=1 iff there is a bounded admissible coded suffix starting at a.
The actual PR4 iterator and PR3 code output have proved length, start,
lookups and admissibility, with a separate PR2 bound depending only on B,
horizon and starting level. Both directions use genuine QF invariants;
the converse accepts arbitrary numerical codes.
[Coded stronger-evidence transport](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/CodedChainRefinement.lean)
now derives validity of the same numerical code from bounded object formulas
comparing sg-normalized local and edge tests. Shared candidate bounds and
horizon preserve the actual code bound; produced/returned chains, bounded
existence and the success bit transfer. Actual PR3/PR4 bounded products
conjoin an internal number of test requirements and derive every indexed
requirement on the decoded chain, including the empty product. Existence
under added constraints is not inferred. The remaining worked-example
claims still require separate proofs.

The [computed Gaussian-name disk](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianNameBounds.lean)
is ceil(L1(f(0,x)))+2. The original Euclidean point-Cauchy condition alone
derives a uniform bound on every actual sample, without a supplied disk or
canonical-input premise. Its PR1 radius and PR2 sample/radius output have
proved returned-field and norm bounds.
[Actual Gaussian-name addition and multiplication](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianNameArithmetic.lean)
now operate on the original samples with separate packed data. Their raw
regularity moduli, two and the sum of the computed input disks, follow from
the original point-Cauchy conditions alone. The actual normalized PR2 output
programs are canonical fast names. Equivalent original inputs produce
cross-equivalent outputs even when computed disks and normalization shifts
differ. Actual raw algebra and finite-slack transport derive these claims;
no output regularity or disk invariant is supplied.
[The actual name ring laws](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianNameRingLaws.lean)
now derive associativity and commutativity for both operations, both
distributions, zero/one identities and additive inverses up to exact
cross-precision equivalence. Actual constant and normalized negative-name
programs are fast, with proved negation properness. A proved finite envelope
comparison removes arbitrary diagonal constants, so every nested computed
normalization shift is retained. Final ring-law hypotheses are only the
original point-Cauchy conditions.
[The actual reciprocal-name program](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianNameReciprocal.lean)
now uses the original finite h+2 dyadic apartness witness. Point CN and that
witness derive tail separation, fractional reciprocal bounds and the
computed R-squared modulus. The actual output is canonical and fast; both
actual multiplication programs give the one-name class, and equivalent
inputs give equivalent inverses even with different apartness witnesses
and shifts. No apartness precision is selected from bare negated zero.
[Uniform polynomial-name Horner interpretation](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianPolynomialNameHorner.lean)
uses one actual PR2 prefix program with numerical prefix length, original
degree and coefficient/point data. The original computed T bounds every
valid prefix; properness and the actual add/multiply recurrence are derived.
The empty prefix is equivalent to zero, and the full prefix retains the
original generic value program's degree, data, disk and shift, including
its exact zero predicate. A computed point disk removes the external disk
premise. The [collected name-level FTA certificate](Lean/IPRADerivations/Analysis/GaussianRepresentedFTA.lean)
now derives the actual trajectory root's point CN, original R disk, every
valid prefix's properness and arithmetic recurrence, and the full Horner
value's program-zero and exact zero-name class from the original source
input alone. The original coefficient data, root data, R, T and shifts are
retained. Its actual PR4 sample output returns the root, value and radius
with derived bounds and canonicality; the nonconstant coefficient index
is proof-only. No RD, root/disk/value/prefix/output law is supplied.
Completed-field realization and represented factorization remain separate.

[The actual stored filtered list](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/FilteredEnumerationCode.lean)
now encodes all increasing rank/select outputs in the checked sequence
format. Its PR2 producer and decoder have proved length, retained bounded
entries, completeness and strict order. Genuine QF inductions prove the
count is at most the original numerical cutoff and bound the actual code
by a separate PR2 pairing-stack bound, including empty cutoffs and lists.
These conclusions use no selector/list/output-law premise. Converting a
general height certificate into a numerical code cutoff remains separate.

The square-root worked example now has an
[actual stored candidate list](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/SquareRootRelationalCandidates.lean)
at exactly the source cutoff ceil((u/v+1)2^n)+1. Its computed predecessor of
the least crossing is retained, all returned rational candidates satisfy
the original (2u/v+3)2^-n residual, and consecutive computed witnesses
satisfy the original 3*2^-n compatibility. The source proof's strict upper
bracket is corrected to a nonstrict auxiliary bracket at perfect squares;
the residual and nonemptiness conclusions are unchanged.
The [finite-horizon certificate](Lean/IPRADerivations/Foundations/SquareRootRelationalCertificate.lean)
uses actual list indices and derives an object-admissible witness code from
v>=1 alone. The genuine backward viability computation then proves success
of its independent automatic selector. Its actual PR3 output has a verified
length-h+1 chain, original rational residual and compatibility at every
valid returned query, and independent chain/tag size bounds. All input
numerators, denominators, horizons and levels are internal; no successful
search, path or viability premise is supplied. Infinite branches and
selection from arbitrary represented input remain separate.
Generic completed Gaussian-point/field realization, coded-field norm/fiber/
annihilator claims, Minkowski physical-basis transport, remaining FIBER
compiler/adapters, Gamma/Barnes analytic identities and all other source
packet obligations remain in scope. Completed-real direction interpretation,
general convolution and the separately expanded telescoping sum remain
unclaimed. The complete 115-packet goal
remains active.

The new modules are included in `IPRADerivations`; all their public declarations
are audited in `Checks/Axioms.lean`. Run the root verification commands:

```sh
lake build IPRADerivations +IPRADerivations.Checks.Axioms
lake env lean --run Logic/PrimitiveRecursiveArithmetic/Bridge/check-import-closure.lean
```
